Recalling the definitions of
\alpha,\beta,\gamma
in (
2.4
) (the distinction between
(\alpha_{t},\beta_{t},\gamma_{t})
and
(\alpha^{\star},\beta^{\star},\gamma^{\star})
is solely due to the evaluation at
(\mu_{t},\nu_{t})
v.s.
(\mu^{\star},\nu^{\star})
), we regard them as functions of
(\mu,\nu)
as follows:

\alpha(\mu,\nu)=\frac{1}{1+\gamma\nu},\quad\beta(\mu,\nu)=1-\sqrt{\frac{\mu}{\nu}},\quad\gamma(\mu,\nu)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu\nu}}.

Each of
\alpha,\beta,\gamma
is continuously differentiable on
(0,\infty)^{2}
. As shown in (
2.6
) and Assumption
3.4
,
(\mu_{t},\nu_{t})\in[\gamma_{S},1]\times[1,1/\gamma_{S}]
,
\forall t\geq 0
. Since
\nabla\alpha,\nabla\beta,\nabla\gamma
are continuous,
\nabla\alpha(\mu,\nu),\nabla\beta(\mu,\nu),\nabla\gamma(\mu,\nu)
are bounded on the compact set
[\gamma_{S},1]\times[1,1/\gamma_{S}]
. Moreover, since
[\gamma_{S},1]\times[1,1/\gamma_{S}]
is convex, we know from the mean value theorem that

|\alpha_{t}-\alpha^{\star}|\lesssim|\mu_{t}-\mu^{\star}|+|\nu_{t}-\nu^{\star}|,\quad|\beta_{t}-\beta^{\star}|\lesssim|\mu_{t}-\mu^{\star}|+|\nu_{t}-\nu^{\star}|,\quad|\gamma_{t}-\gamma^{\star}|\lesssim|\mu_{t}-\mu^{\star}|+|\nu_{t}-\nu^{\star}|.

(B.2)

Thus, it suffices to show
(\mu_{t},\nu_{t})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a.s.}}{{\rightarrow}}(\mu^{\star},\nu^{\star})
. To that end, let us start with defining several matrices as follows:

\displaystyle\widetilde{Z}_{t}

\displaystyle\coloneqq B_{t}S(S^{\top}B_{t}^{2}S)^{\dagger}S^{\top}B_{t},\quad

\displaystyle Z_{t}\coloneqq\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{Z}_{t}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}],

(B.3)

\displaystyle\widetilde{Z}^{\star}

\displaystyle\coloneqq B^{\star}S(S^{\top}(B^{\star})^{2}S)^{\dagger}S^{\top}B^{\star},\quad

\displaystyle Z^{\star}\coloneqq\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{Z}^{\star}].

We next state a lemma characterizing the continuity of the projection matrix
\widetilde{Z}_{t}=B_{t}S(S^{\top}B_{t}^{2}S)^{\dagger}S^{\top}B_{t}
with respect to the Hessian estimate
B_{t}
.

Lemma B.1
.

(
41
, Lemma 5.2)

Suppose
B_{t},B^{\star}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
are non-singular. Then for any
S\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times s}
, we have

\|\tilde{Z}_{t}-\tilde{Z}^{\star}\|\leq\frac{2\|B_{t}-B^{\star}\|}{\sigma_{\min}(B^{\star})}\cdot\|S\|\|S^{\dagger}\|,

where
\sigma_{\min}(\cdot)
denotes the least singular value.

By Lemma
B.1
, the discrepancy between the projection matrices induced by the Hessian estimates can be bounded as

\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{Z}_{t}-\widetilde{Z}^{\star}\|\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\leq\frac{2\|B_{t}-B^{\star}\|}{\gamma_{H}}\,\mathbb{E}\!\left[\|S\|\,\|S^{\dagger}\|\right]\;\lesssim\;\|B_{t}-B^{\star}\|.

(B.4)

Here, the first inequality follows from Lemma
B.1
together with the independence between
S
and
\mathcal{F}_{t-1}
implied by Assumption
3.4
, while the second one is a direct consequence of Assumption
3.4
with
q_{S}=1
.

\bullet\bullet

Convergence of
\mu_{t}
.

By the definitions of
\mu_{t}
in (
2.5
) and
\mu^{\star}
in Section
4.1
, we have
\mu_{t}=\lambda_{\min}(Z_{t})
and
\mu^{\star}=\lambda_{\min}(Z^{\star})
. Applying Weyl’s perturbation theorem
(
3
, Corollary III.2.6)
, we obtain

\displaystyle|\mu_{t}-\mu^{\star}|

\displaystyle\leq\|Z_{t}-Z^{\star}\|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{def:Z_t}}}{{=}}\|\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{Z}_{t}-\widetilde{Z}^{\star}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\|\leq\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{Z}_{t}-\widetilde{Z}^{\star}\|\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{equ:B_bound_projection}}}{{\lesssim}}\|B_{t}-B^{\star}\|,

(B.5)

where the third inequality follows from Jensen’s inequality. Since
B_{t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a.s.}}{{\rightarrow}}B^{\star}
, we conclude that
\mu_{t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a.s.}}{{\rightarrow}}\mu^{\star}
as
t\rightarrow\infty
.

\bullet\bullet

Convergence of
\nu_{t}
.

Recall the definition of
\nu_{t}
in (
2.5
) and define
A_{t}\coloneqq\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{Z}_{t}Z_{t}^{-1}\widetilde{Z}_{t}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]
. The matrix
A_{t}
is symmetric positive semidefinite, since
Z_{t}^{-1}\succeq I
and both
\widetilde{Z}_{t}
and
Z_{t}
are symmetric. Letting
\bm{b}=Z_{t}^{1/2}\bm{a}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\nu_{t}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{def:mu_t_nu_t}}}}{{=}}\sup_{\bm{a}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\backslash\{\bm{0}\}}\frac{\langle A_{t}\bm{a},\bm{a}\rangle}{\langle Z_{t}\bm{a},\bm{a}\rangle}=\sup_{\bm{b}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\backslash\{\bm{0}\}}\frac{\langle A_{t}Z_{t}^{-1/2}\bm{b},Z_{t}^{-1/2}\bm{b}\rangle}{\langle Z_{t}Z_{t}^{-1/2}\bm{b},Z_{t}^{-1/2}\bm{b}\rangle}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\bm{b}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\backslash\{\bm{0}\}}\frac{\langle Z_{t}^{-1/2}A_{t}Z_{t}^{-1/2}\bm{b},\bm{b}\rangle}{\langle\bm{b},\bm{b}\rangle}=\lambda_{\max}(Z_{t}^{-1/2}A_{t}Z_{t}^{-1/2})=\|Z_{t}^{-1/2}A_{t}Z_{t}^{-1/2}\|,

where
\lambda_{\max}(\cdot)
denotes the largest eigenvalue. Applying the same argument to
\nu^{\star}
yields
\nu^{\star}=\|(Z^{\star})^{-1/2}A^{\star}(Z^{\star})^{-1/2}\|
with
A^{\star}\coloneqq\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{Z}^{\star}(Z^{\star})^{-1}\widetilde{Z}^{\star}]
, where the expectation is taken only over the sketching distribution
S
. Consequently, we have

\displaystyle|\nu_{t}-\nu^{\star}|=\left|\|Z_{t}^{-1/2}A_{t}Z_{t}^{-1/2}\|-\|(Z^{\star})^{-1/2}A^{\star}(Z^{\star})^{-1/2}\|\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|Z_{t}^{-1/2}A_{t}Z_{t}^{-1/2}-(Z^{\star})^{-1/2}A^{\star}(Z^{\star})^{-1/2}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|(Z_{t}^{-1/2}-(Z^{\star})^{-1/2})A_{t}Z_{t}^{-1/2}\|+\|(Z^{\star})^{-1/2}(A_{t}-A^{\star})Z_{t}^{-1/2}\|+\|(Z^{\star})^{-1/2}A^{\star}(Z_{t}^{-1/2}-(Z^{\star})^{-1/2})\|.

(B.6)

To control the three terms on the right-hand side of (
B.1
), we first introduce the following preparation lemma.

Lemma B.2
.

Under the conditions of Lemma
4.1
, we have

\|Z_{t}^{-1}-(Z^{\star})^{-1}\|\leq\frac{1}{\gamma_{S}^{2}}\|Z_{t}-Z^{\star}\|,\quad\quad\quad\|Z_{t}^{-1/2}-(Z^{\star})^{-1/2}\|\leq\frac{1}{2\gamma_{S}^{3/2}}\|Z_{t}-Z^{\star}\|.

First term in (
B.1
).

By Assumption
3.4
and the fact that
\widetilde{Z}_{t}
is a projection matrix, we have
\|A_{t}\|\leq\gamma_{S}^{-1}
and
\|Z_{t}^{-1/2}\|\leq\gamma_{S}^{-1/2}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\|(Z_{t}^{-1/2}-(Z^{\star})^{-1/2})A_{t}Z_{t}^{-1/2}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|Z_{t}^{-1/2}-(Z^{\star})^{-1/2}\|\|A_{t}\|\|Z_{t}^{-1/2}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\gamma_{S}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\gamma_{S}}}\frac{1}{2\gamma_{S}^{3/2}}\|Z_{t}-Z^{\star}\|=\frac{1}{2\gamma_{S}^{3}}\|Z_{t}-Z^{\star}\|,

(B.7)

where the second last inequality follows from Lemma
B.2
.

Third term in (
B.1
).

Similarly, since
\|A^{\star}\|\leq\gamma_{S}^{-1}
and
\|(Z^{\star})^{-1/2}\|\leq\gamma_{S}^{-1/2}
, we again apply Lemma
B.2
and obtain

\|(Z^{\star})^{-1/2}A^{\star}(Z_{t}^{-1/2}-(Z^{\star})^{-1/2})\|\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\gamma_{S}}}\frac{1}{\gamma_{S}}\frac{1}{2\gamma_{S}^{3/2}}\|Z_{t}-Z^{\star}\|=\frac{1}{2\gamma_{S}^{3}}\|Z_{t}-Z^{\star}\|.

(B.8)

Second term in (
B.1
).

Using
\|(Z^{\star})^{-1/2}\|\leq\gamma_{S}^{-1/2}
and
\|Z_{t}^{-1/2}\|\leq\gamma_{S}^{-1/2}
, we have

\displaystyle\|(Z^{\star})^{-1/2}(A_{t}-A^{\star})Z_{t}^{-1/2}\|\leq\frac{1}{\gamma_{S}}\|A_{t}-A^{\star}\|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\gamma_{S}}\left\|\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{Z}_{t}Z_{t}^{-1}\widetilde{Z}_{t}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]-\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{Z}^{\star}(Z^{\star})^{-1}\widetilde{Z}^{\star}]\right\|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\gamma_{S}}\left\|\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{Z}_{t}Z_{t}^{-1}\widetilde{Z}_{t}-\widetilde{Z}^{\star}(Z^{\star})^{-1}\widetilde{Z}^{\star}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\right\|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\gamma_{S}}\left\|\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{Z}_{t}-\widetilde{Z}^{\star})Z_{t}^{-1}\widetilde{Z}_{t}+\widetilde{Z}^{\star}(Z_{t}^{-1}-(Z^{\star})^{-1})\widetilde{Z}_{t}+\widetilde{Z}^{\star}(Z^{\star})^{-1}(\widetilde{Z}_{t}-\widetilde{Z}^{\star})\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\gamma_{S}}(\mathbb{E}[\|(\widetilde{Z}_{t}-\widetilde{Z}^{\star})Z_{t}^{-1}\widetilde{Z}_{t}\|\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]+\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{Z}^{\star}(Z_{t}^{-1}-(Z^{\star})^{-1})\widetilde{Z}_{t}\|\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]+\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{Z}^{\star}(Z^{\star})^{-1}(\widetilde{Z}_{t}-\widetilde{Z}^{\star})\|\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}])

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\gamma_{S}}\left(\frac{1}{\gamma_{S}}\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{Z}_{t}-\widetilde{Z}^{\star}\|\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]+\|Z_{t}^{-1}-(Z^{\star})^{-1}\|+\frac{1}{\gamma_{S}}\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{Z}_{t}-\widetilde{Z}^{\star}\|\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\gamma_{S}}\left(\frac{1}{\gamma_{S}}\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{Z}_{t}-\widetilde{Z}^{\star}\|\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]+\frac{1}{\gamma_{S}^{2}}\|Z_{t}-Z^{\star}\|+\frac{1}{\gamma_{S}}\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{Z}_{t}-\widetilde{Z}^{\star}\|\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\gamma_{S}^{3}}\|Z_{t}-Z^{\star}\|+\frac{2}{\gamma_{S}^{2}}\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{Z}_{t}-\widetilde{Z}^{\star}\|\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}].

(B.9)

Plugging (
B.1
), (
B.8
), (
B.1
) into (
B.1
) gives us

\displaystyle|\nu_{t}-\nu^{\star}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{\gamma_{S}^{3}}\|Z_{t}-Z^{\star}\|+\frac{2}{\gamma_{S}^{2}}\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{Z}_{t}-\widetilde{Z}^{\star}\|\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\gamma_{S}^{3}}\left\|\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{Z}_{t}-\widetilde{Z}^{\star}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\right\|+\frac{2}{\gamma_{S}^{2}}\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{Z}_{t}-\widetilde{Z}^{\star}\|\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{\gamma_{S}^{3}}\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{Z}_{t}-\widetilde{Z}^{\star}\|\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]+\frac{2}{\gamma_{S}^{2}}\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{Z}_{t}-\widetilde{Z}^{\star}\|\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{Z}_{t}-\widetilde{Z}^{\star}\|\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{equ:B_bound_projection}}}{{\lesssim}}\|B_{t}-B^{\star}\|.

(B.10)

Since
B_{t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a.s.}}{{\rightarrow}}B^{\star}
, we conclude that
\nu_{t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a.s.}}{{\rightarrow}}\nu^{\star}
as
t\rightarrow\infty
. Combining (
B.5
) and (
B.1
) with (
B.2
) yields

|\alpha_{t}-\alpha^{\star}|\lesssim\|B_{t}-B^{\star}\|,\quad\quad|\beta_{t}-\beta^{\star}|\lesssim\|B_{t}-B^{\star}\|,\quad\quad|\gamma_{t}-\gamma^{\star}|\lesssim\|B_{t}-B^{\star}\|,

(B.11)

which implies that
(\alpha_{t},\beta_{t},\gamma_{t})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a.s.}}{{\rightarrow}}(\alpha^{\star},\beta^{\star},\gamma^{\star})
as
t\rightarrow\infty
.

\bullet

Convergence of
K_{t}
.
Recall the constructions of
K_{t}
and
K^{\star}
from
C_{t}
and
C^{\star}
in (
3.2
). To establish the convergence of
K_{t}
, it suffices to study the convergence of
C_{t}
. Due to the independence among the sketching matrices, both
C_{t}
and
C^{\star}
admit the formulas
C_{t}=(M_{t})^{\tau}
and
C^{\star}=(M^{\star})^{\tau}
, where
M_{t}
is defined in (
A.16
) and
M^{\star}
is defined analogously by replacing
\alpha_{t},\beta_{t},\gamma_{t}
and
Z_{t}
with their counterparts evaluated at
\bm{x}^{\star}
. We first bound
\|M_{t}-M^{\star}\|
as follows:

\displaystyle\|M_{t}-M^{\star}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|(1-\alpha_{t})(I-Z_{t})-(1-\alpha^{\star})(I-Z^{\star})\|+\|\alpha_{t}(I-Z_{t})-\alpha^{\star}(I-Z^{\star})\|

\displaystyle\quad+\|(1-\alpha_{t})(1-\beta_{t})I-(1-\alpha_{t})\gamma_{t}Z_{t}-(1-\alpha^{\star})(1-\beta^{\star})I+(1-\alpha^{\star})\gamma^{\star}Z^{\star}\|

\displaystyle\quad+\|(\alpha_{t}+\beta_{t}-\alpha_{t}\beta_{t})I-\alpha_{t}\gamma_{t}Z_{t}-(\alpha^{\star}+\beta^{\star}-\alpha^{\star}\beta^{\star})I+\alpha^{\star}\gamma^{\star}Z^{\star}\|

\displaystyle\lesssim|\alpha_{t}-\alpha^{\star}|+|\beta_{t}-\beta^{\star}|+|\gamma_{t}-\gamma^{\star}|+\|Z_{t}-Z^{\star}\|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{equ:alpha_beta_gamma_bounds}}}}{{\lesssim}}\;\|B_{t}-B^{\star}\|+\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{Z}_{t}-\widetilde{Z}^{\star}\|\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{equ:B_bound_projection}}}{{\lesssim}}\|B_{t}-B^{\star}\|.

(B.12)

Here, the second inequality uses the bounds
\alpha_{t}<1
,
\beta_{t}<1
,
\gamma_{t}\leq 1/\sqrt{\gamma_{S}}
, and
\|Z_{t}\|\leq 1
, and the third inequality uses the fact that
\|Z_{t}-Z^{\star}\|=\|\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{Z}_{t}-\widetilde{Z}^{\star}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\|\leq\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{Z}_{t}-\widetilde{Z}^{\star}\|\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]
. Combining (
B.1
) with a telescoping expansion, we obtain

\displaystyle\|C_{t}-C^{\star}\|

\displaystyle=\|(M_{t})^{\tau}-(M^{\star})^{\tau}\|=\left\|\sum_{k=0}^{\tau-1}M_{t}^{\tau-1-k}(M_{t}-M^{\star})(M^{\star})^{k}\right\|\leq\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\tau-1}\|M_{t}\|^{\tau-1-k}\|M^{\star}\|^{k}\right)\|M_{t}-M^{\star}\|.

For
M_{t}
, we find the following uniform upper bound:

\displaystyle\|M_{t}\|

\displaystyle\;\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{equ:def_M_t}}}}{{\leq}}\;\|I-Z_{t}\|+(1-\alpha_{t})(1-\beta_{t})+(1-\alpha_{t})\gamma_{t}\|Z_{t}\|+(\alpha_{t}+\beta_{t}-\alpha_{t}\beta_{t})+\alpha_{t}\gamma_{t}\|Z_{t}\|

\displaystyle\;\leq\;\|I-Z_{t}\|+1+\gamma_{t}\|Z_{t}\|\leq 1+1+\gamma_{t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{prop_gamma_t}}}}{{\leq}}2+1/\sqrt{\gamma_{S}}.

By the almost-sure convergence of
B_{t}
,
Z_{t}
, and
(\alpha_{t},\beta_{t},\gamma_{t})
, the same bound holds for
\|M^{\star}\|
. Consequently, the above two displays lead to

\|C_{t}-C^{\star}\|\leq(2+1/\sqrt{\gamma_{S}})^{\tau-1}\cdot\tau\cdot\|M_{t}-M^{\star}\|\lesssim\|M_{t}-M^{\star}\|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{equ:M_bound}}}{{\lesssim}}\|B_{t}-B^{\star}\|.

(B.13)

Finally, by the definitions of
K_{t}
and
K^{\star}
, we have

\|K_{t}-K^{\star}\|=\left\|\begin{pmatrix}I&\bm{0}\end{pmatrix}(C_{t}-C^{\star})\begin{pmatrix}I\\
I\end{pmatrix}\right\|\leq\sqrt{2}\cdot\|C_{t}-C^{\star}\|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{equ:103}}}{{\lesssim}}\|B_{t}-B^{\star}\|.

(B.14)

Since
B_{t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a.s.}}{{\rightarrow}}B^{\star}
, we conclude that
K_{t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a.s.}}{{\rightarrow}}K^{\star}
as
t\rightarrow\infty
.

B.2
Proof of Lemma
B.2

For the first result, we have

\|Z_{t}^{-1}-(Z^{\star})^{-1}\|=\|Z_{t}^{-1}(Z_{t}-Z^{\star})(Z^{\star})^{-1}\|\leq\|Z_{t}^{-1}\|\|Z_{t}-Z^{\star}\|\|(Z^{\star})^{-1}\|\leq\frac{1}{\gamma_{S}^{2}}\|Z_{t}-Z^{\star}\|,

where the last inequality follows from
Z_{t}\succeq\gamma_{S}I
and
Z^{\star}\succeq\gamma_{S}I
in Assumption
3.4
, together with the almost sure convergence
Z_{t}\xrightarrow{a.s.}Z^{\star}
established in Theorem
3.8
and Lemma
B.1
.

For the second result, we proceed in two steps.

\bullet

Step 1.

We show that for any positive definite matrix
X
,

X^{-1/2}=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{-1/2}(X+tI)^{-1}\,dt.

Indeed, for any scalar
x>0
, the following identity holds

x^{-1/2}=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{-1/2}(x+t)^{-1}\,dt.

To verify this, let
t=xu^{2}
, so that
dt=2xu\,du
. Then

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{-1/2}(x+t)^{-1}dt

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}(xu^{2})^{-1/2}(x+xu^{2})^{-1}2xudu

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{2}{x^{1/2}(1+u^{2})}du=\frac{2}{\pi x^{1/2}}\cdot\left.\text{arctan}(u)\right|_{0}^{\infty}=x^{-1/2}.

(B.15)

We now extend this identity to matrices. Since
X
is positive definite, it admits an eigendecomposition
X=UDU^{\top}
, where
D
is diagonal and
U
is orthogonal. Then
X^{-1/2}=UD^{-1/2}U^{\top}
, and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{-1/2}(X+tI)^{-1}dt

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{-1/2}(UDU^{\top}+tUIU^{\top})^{-1}dt=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{-1/2}(U(D+tI)U^{\top})^{-1}dt

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{-1/2}U(D+tI)^{-1}U^{\top}dt=U\left(\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{-1/2}(D+tI)^{-1}dt\right)U^{\top}.

(B.16)

Since
(D+tI)^{-1}
is diagonal, the integral is evaluated entry-wise. By (
B.2
), the
i
th diagonal entry equals
\lambda_{i}^{-1/2}
, where
\lambda_{i}
is the
i
-th eigenvalue of
X
. Thus, we have

\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{-1/2}(X+tI)^{-1}dt\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{equ:94}}}{{=}}U\left(\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{-1/2}(D+tI)^{-1}dt\right)U^{\top}=UD^{-1/2}U^{\top}=X^{-1/2}.

\bullet

Step 2.

By the representation from Step 1, we can write

\displaystyle\|Z_{t}^{-1/2}-(Z^{\star})^{-1/2}\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}y^{-1/2}\left[(Z_{t}+yI)^{-1}-(Z^{\star}+yI)^{-1}\right]dy\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}y^{-1/2}(Z_{t}+yI)^{-1}(Z^{\star}-Z_{t})(Z^{\star}+yI)^{-1}dy\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\|Z^{\star}-Z_{t}\|}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}y^{-1/2}\|(Z_{t}+yI)^{-1}\|\|(Z^{\star}+yI)^{-1}\|dy

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\|Z^{\star}-Z_{t}\|}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}y^{-1/2}\frac{1}{(\gamma_{S}+y)^{2}}dy,

where the last inequality uses
Z_{t}\succeq\gamma_{S}I
and
Z^{\star}\succeq\gamma_{S}I
. To evaluate the integral, set
y=\gamma_{S}u^{2}
, which yields

\int_{0}^{\infty}y^{-1/2}\frac{1}{(\gamma_{S}+y)^{2}}dy=\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{(\gamma_{S}u^{2})^{-1/2}}{(\gamma_{S}+\gamma_{S}u^{2})^{2}}(2\gamma_{S}udu)=\frac{2}{\gamma_{S}^{3/2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(1+u^{2})^{2}}du.

Letting
u=\tan\theta
further gives us

\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(1+u^{2})^{2}}du=\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}\frac{\text{sec}^{2}\theta}{(1+\text{tan}^{2}\theta)^{2}}d\theta=\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}\frac{1}{\text{sec}^{2}\theta}d\theta=\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}\text{cos}^{2}\theta d\theta=\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}\frac{1+\text{cos}2\theta}{2}d\theta=\frac{\pi}{4}.

Combining the above three displays, we conclude that

\|Z_{t}^{-1/2}-(Z^{\star})^{-1/2}\|\leq\frac{\|Z^{\star}-Z_{t}\|}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}y^{-1/2}\frac{1}{(\gamma_{S}+y)^{2}}dy=\frac{1}{2\gamma_{S}^{3/2}}\|Z^{\star}-Z_{t}\|.

This completes the proof.

B.3
Proof of Lemma
4.2

To prove this lemma, we have to localize the analysis by introducing the following stopping time: for any given
k\geq 0
,
r>0
,

\tau_{k,r}\coloneqq\inf\left\{t\geq k:\|\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|>r\;\;\text{OR}\;\;\|B_{t}-B^{\star}\|>r\right\}.

(B.17)

It is a stopping time since for any
t\geq k
,

\{\tau_{k,r}\leq t\}=\bigcup_{s=k}^{t}\Big(\{\|\bm{x}_{s}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|>r\}\cup\{\|B_{s}-B^{\star}\|>r\}\Big)\in\mathcal{F}_{t-1},

where the last inclusion uses the fact that
(\bm{x}_{s},B_{s})
is
\mathcal{F}_{s-1}
-measurable for any
s\geq 1
. Furthermore, since
\bm{x}_{t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a.s.}}{{\rightarrow}}\bm{x}^{\star}
and
B_{t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a.s.}}{{\rightarrow}}B^{\star}
given in Theorem
3.8
and Lemma
4.1
, respectively, we know there exists a random variable
T_{r}
with
\mathbb{P}(T_{r}<\infty)=1
such that, for any realization of the sample sequence,
\|\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|\leq r
and
\|B_{t}-B^{\star}\|\leq r
for any
t\geq T_{r}
. For this realization, we know that if
k\geq T_{r}
, then
\tau_{k,r}=\infty
. Therefore, we conclude that

\mathbb{P}(\tau_{k,r}<\infty)\leq\mathbb{P}(T_{r}>k)\rightarrow 0\quad\text{ as }\quad k\rightarrow\infty.

This suggests that for any given
\epsilon,r>0
, we can choose
k=k(\epsilon,r)
(depending on
\epsilon,r
) such that
\mathbb{P}(\tau_{k,r}<\infty)\leq 0.5\epsilon
. Furthermore, for any
t\geq k
, we have

\mathbb{P}(\tau_{k,r}\leq t)\leq\mathbb{P}(\tau_{k,r}<\infty)<\frac{\epsilon}{2}.

(B.18)

Next, we introduce how we choose
r>0
. To this end, we introduce the following decomposition of the iterate error
\Delta_{t}\coloneqq\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star}
.

Lemma B.3
.

The iterate error
\Delta_{t}
of the algorithm scheme (
2.8
) can be decomposed as

\Delta_{t+1}=(I-\varphi_{t}\mathcal{R})\Delta_{t}+\varphi_{t}\bm{\theta}_{t}+\varphi_{t}\bm{\delta}_{t},

(B.19)

where
\mathcal{R}\coloneqq I-K^{\star}
,
(\bm{\theta}_{t},\mathcal{F}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
is a martingale difference sequence and
\bm{\delta}_{t}
is the error term, which are defined by

\displaystyle\bm{\theta}_{t}

\displaystyle\coloneqq-(I-K_{t})B_{t}^{-1}(g_{t}-\nabla f_{t})+(\bm{z}_{t,\tau}-(I-K_{t})\Delta\bm{x}_{t}),

(B.20a)

\displaystyle\bm{\delta}_{t}

\displaystyle\coloneqq(K_{t}-K^{\star})\Delta_{t}-(I-K_{t})\left[(B^{\star})^{-1}(\nabla f_{t}-B^{\star}\Delta_{t})\right]-(I-K_{t})\left[B_{t}^{-1}-(B^{\star})^{-1}\right]\nabla f_{t}.

(B.20b)

In addition, by Lemma
3.7
and the condition on
\tau
that
\tau(1-\sqrt{\mu_{t}/\nu_{t}})^{\tau-2}\leq\gamma_{H}/(4\Upsilon_{H})
, we know
\|K_{t}\|\leq\gamma_{H}/(2\Upsilon_{H})<0.5
. By the convergence of
K_{t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a.s.}}{{\rightarrow}}K^{\star}
in Lemma
4.1
,
\|K^{\star}\|\leq\gamma_{H}/(2\Upsilon_{H})<0.5
. By the definition of
\bm{\delta}_{t}
in (
B.20b
), we have

\displaystyle\|\bm{\delta}_{t}\|\;

\displaystyle\leq\;\frac{3}{2}\left(\|(B^{\star})^{-1}\|\cdot\|\nabla f_{t}-B^{\star}(\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star})\|+\|B_{t}^{-1}-(B^{\star})^{-1}\|\cdot\|\nabla f_{t}\|\right)+\|K_{t}-K^{\star}\|\cdot\|\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{equ:K_t-Kstar}}}}{{\lesssim}}\;\frac{3\Upsilon_{L}}{2\gamma_{H}}\|\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{2}+\frac{3}{2\gamma_{H}^{2}}\|B_{t}-B^{\star}\|\cdot\|\nabla f_{t}\|+\|B_{t}-B^{\star}\|\cdot\|\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|,

(B.21)

where the first inequality is due to
\|K_{t}\|<0.5
and the second inequality also uses Assumptions
3.1
and
3.2
. By the
\Upsilon_{H}
-Lipschitz continuity of
\nabla f(\bm{x})
as implied by Assumption
3.1
, (
B.3
) directly implies that there exists
C_{\delta}>0
such that

\|\bm{\delta}_{t}\|\leq C_{\delta}\|\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{2}+C_{\delta}\|B_{t}-B^{\star}\|\cdot\|\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|,\quad\forall t\geq 0.

(B.22)

Let
\lambda_{m}\coloneqq\lambda_{\min}(\mathcal{R})>0.5
. By the condition on
C_{\varphi}
that
2C_{\varphi}\lambda_{m}>1
when
\varphi=1
, we choose
\eta>0
such that
2C_{\varphi}\lambda_{m}(1-\eta)>1
. Then, for such an
\eta>0
, we choose
r>0
small enough to satisfy

2C_{\delta}r\leq\eta\lambda_{m}\quad\text{ and }\quad 4C_{\delta}^{2}r^{2}\leq\eta\lambda_{m}.

(B.23)

With the above chosen
r>0
and any given
\epsilon>0
, by (
B.18
), we can choose
k=k(\epsilon)
large enough so that
\mathbb{P}(\tau_{k,r}\leq t)<0.5\epsilon
for any
t\geq k
. Here,
r
has been chosen as a constant and hence the dependency of
k
on
r
is suppressed.

With this result, we next establish the convergence rates in probability of
B_{t}
,
\alpha_{t}
,
\beta_{t}
,
\gamma_{t}
, and
K_{t}
separately.

\bullet

Convergence rate of
B_{t}
.

We follow the above discussion. To obtain
O_{p}(\sqrt{\varphi_{t}})
rate for
\|B_{t}-B^{\star}\|
, for any given
\epsilon>0
and any
M>0
, we investigate the following quantity

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\!\left(\|B_{t}-B^{\star}\|>M\sqrt{\varphi_{t}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\!\left(\|B_{t}-B^{\star}\|^{2}>M^{2}\varphi_{t}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\!\left(\{\|B_{t}-B^{\star}\|^{2}>M^{2}\varphi_{t}\}\cap\{\tau_{k,r}>t\}\right)+\mathbb{P}\!\left(\{\|B_{t}-B^{\star}\|^{2}>M^{2}\varphi_{t}\}\cap\{\tau_{k,r}\leq t\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\!\left(\|B_{t}-B^{\star}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>t\}}>M^{2}\varphi_{t}\right)+\mathbb{P}(\tau_{k,r}\leq t)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}[\|B_{t}-B^{\star}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>t\}}]}{M^{2}\varphi_{t}}+\frac{\epsilon}{2}.

(B.24)

By the above derivation, we see that it suffices to show
\mathbb{E}[\|B_{t}-B^{\star}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>t\}}]\leq C_{B}\,\varphi_{t}
for
t
large enough with some constant
C_{B}>0
. Then, we can choose
M=M(\epsilon)=\sqrt{2C_{B}/\epsilon}
such that
\mathbb{P}\!\left(\|B_{t}-B^{\star}\|>M\sqrt{\varphi_{t}}\right)\leq\epsilon
, which further implies
\|B_{t}-B^{\star}\|=O_{p}(\sqrt{\varphi_{t}})
.

By (
B.1
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|B_{t}-B^{\star}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>t\}}]

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}(H_{i}-\nabla^{2}f_{i})\right\|^{2}\right]+2\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{\Upsilon_{L}}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\|\bm{x}_{i}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|\right)^{2}\bm{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>t\}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}(H_{i}-\nabla^{2}f_{i})\right\|^{2}\right]+\frac{2\Upsilon_{L}^{2}}{t^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\|\bm{x}_{i}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|\bm{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>i\}}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}(H_{i}-\nabla^{2}f_{i})\right\|^{2}\right]+\frac{2\Upsilon_{L}^{2}}{t^{2}}\left(\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{x}_{i}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>i\}}\right]\right)^{1/2}\right)^{2},

(B.25)

where the second inequality uses the fact
\{\tau_{k,r}>t\}\subseteq\{\tau_{k,r}>i\}
for
i\leq t
, and the last inequality uses the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality.

\bullet\bullet

Convergence rate of the first term in (
B.3
).

To control the first term on the right-hand side, we first establish the bound for
\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{2}]
as follows. We take full expectation on both sides of (
A.32
) and obtain for all
t
large enough,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[f_{t+1}-f^{\star}]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}[f_{t}-f^{\star}]-\frac{\varphi_{t}}{4\Upsilon_{H}}\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla f_{t}\|^{2}]+\frac{\Upsilon_{H}(1+C_{K})^{2}C_{g,2}}{2\gamma_{H}^{2}}\varphi_{t}^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{equ:46}}}}{{\leq}}\;\mathbb{E}[f_{t}-f^{\star}]-\frac{\gamma_{H}\varphi_{t}}{2\Upsilon_{H}}\mathbb{E}[f_{t}-f^{\star}]+\frac{\Upsilon_{H}(1+C_{K})^{2}C_{g,2}}{2\gamma_{H}^{2}}\varphi_{t}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(1-\frac{\gamma_{H}\varphi_{t}}{2\Upsilon_{H}}\right)\mathbb{E}[f_{t}-f^{\star}]+\frac{\Upsilon_{H}(1+C_{K})^{2}C_{g,2}}{2\gamma_{H}^{2}}\varphi_{t}^{2}.

By
34
, we conclude
\limsup_{t\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{E}[f_{t}-f^{\star}]=0
and hence
\mathbb{E}[f_{t}-f^{\star}]\lesssim 1
. Applying (
A.23
) gives us

\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{2}]\leq\frac{2}{\gamma_{H}}\mathbb{E}[f_{t}-f^{\star}]\lesssim 1.

(B.26)

We now return to the first term on the right-hand side of (
B.3
) and obtain

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}(H_{i}-\nabla^{2}f_{i})\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}(H_{i}-\nabla^{2}f_{i})\right\|^{2}_{F}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{t^{2}}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|H_{i}-\nabla^{2}f_{i}\right\|^{2}_{F}\right]\leq\frac{d}{t^{2}}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|H_{i}-\nabla^{2}f_{i}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{t^{2}}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[\|H_{i}-\nabla^{2}f_{i}\|^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]]\leq\frac{d}{t^{2}}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\mathbb{E}[C_{H,1}\|\bm{x}_{i}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{2}+C_{H,2}]\;\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{equ:53}}}}{{\lesssim}}\;\frac{1}{t}.

(B.27)

Here, the second equality uses the conditional orthogonality of martingale differences (via the tower property) under the Frobenius inner product and the second-to-last inequality is implied by Assumption
3.3
with
q_{H}=2
.

\bullet\bullet

Convergence rate of the second term in (
B.3
).

To control the second term on the right-hand side, we first establish the bound for
u_{t}\coloneqq\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Delta_{t}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>t\}}\right]
, where we recall that
\Delta_{t}=\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star}
. By the one-step recursion of
\Delta_{t}
in Lemma
B.3
,
\forall t\geq k
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Delta_{t+1}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>t\}}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|(I-\varphi_{t}\mathcal{R})\Delta_{t}+\varphi_{t}\bm{\theta}_{t}+\varphi_{t}\bm{\delta}_{t}\right\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>t\}}\big|\mathcal{F}_{t-1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq(1-\varphi_{t}\lambda_{m})^{2}\|\Delta_{t}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>t\}}+\varphi_{t}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{\theta}_{t}+\bm{\delta}_{t}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\tau_{k,r}>t}\big|\mathcal{F}_{t-1}\right]+2\varphi_{t}\left\langle(I-\varphi_{t}\mathcal{R})\Delta_{t},\;\bm{\delta}_{t}\,\mathbf{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>t\}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq(1-\varphi_{t}\lambda_{m})^{2}\|\Delta_{t}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>t\}}+2\varphi_{t}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{\theta}_{t}\|^{2}\big|\mathcal{F}_{t-1}\right]+2\varphi_{t}^{2}\|\bm{\delta}_{t}\|^{2}\mathbf{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>t\}}

\displaystyle\quad+2\varphi_{t}\left\langle(I-\varphi_{t}\mathcal{R})\Delta_{t},\;\bm{\delta}_{t}\,\mathbf{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>t\}}\right\rangle,

(B.28)

Here, the second equality holds since
(\bm{\theta}_{t},\mathcal{F}_{t})
is a martingale difference sequence and
\mathbf{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>t\}}
is
\mathcal{F}_{t-1}
-measurable; the third inequality follows from the Young’s inequality. Below we deal with each term on the right-hand side of (
B.3
) and then derive the one-step recursion for
u_{t}=\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Delta_{t}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>t\}}\right]
.

For the
second
term in (
B.3
), we state the following lemma.

Lemma B.4
.

Under the conditions of Lemma
4.2
, for
q=q_{g}>2
, there exist
C_{q,1},C_{q,2}>0
such that

\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{\theta}_{t}\|^{q}\middle|\mathcal{F}_{t-1}\right]\leq C_{q,1}\|\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{q}+C_{q,2}.

By Lemma
B.4
with
q=q_{g}>2
, we have

\mathbb{E}\!\left[\|\bm{\theta}_{t}\|^{2}\big|\mathcal{F}_{t-1}\right]\leq\left(\mathbb{E}\!\left[\|\bm{\theta}_{t}\|^{q_{g}}\big|\mathcal{F}_{t-1}\right]\right)^{2/q_{g}}\leq\left(C_{q_{g},1}\|\Delta_{t}\|^{q_{g}}+C_{q_{g},2}\right)^{2/q_{g}}\leq C_{q_{g},1}^{2/q_{g}}\|\Delta_{t}\|^{2}+C_{q_{g},2}^{2/q_{g}},

(B.29)

Here, the first step follows from the Jensen’s inequality; and the last inequality uses that
2/q_{g}\in(0,1)
and hence
(a+b)^{2/q_{g}}\leq a^{2/q_{g}}+b^{2/q_{g}}
for
a,b\geq 0
.

For the
third
term in (
B.3
), we first obtain from (
B.22
) that

\|\bm{\delta}_{t}\|\,\bm{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>t\}}\leq C_{\delta}r\|\Delta_{t}\|\,\bm{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>t\}}+C_{\delta}r\|\Delta_{t}\|\,\bm{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>t\}}=2C_{\delta}r\|\Delta_{t}\|\,\bm{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>t\}},

(B.30)

where the first inequality applies the definition of the stopping time
\tau_{k,r}
in (
B.17
). Then, by the choice of
r
mentioned in (
B.23
), we have

\|\bm{\delta}_{t}\|^{2}\,\bm{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>t\}}\leq 4C_{\delta}^{2}r^{2}\|\Delta_{t}\|^{2}\,\bm{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>t\}}\leq\eta\lambda_{m}\|\Delta_{t}\|^{2}\,\bm{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>t\}},

(B.31)

where we recall that
\eta
is chosen so that
2C_{\varphi}\lambda_{m}(1-\eta)>1
.

For the
last
term in (
B.3
), we have

\left\langle(I-\varphi_{t}\mathcal{R})\Delta_{t},\;\bm{\delta}_{t}\,\mathbf{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>t\}}\right\rangle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{equ:delta}}}{{\leq}}\|I-\varphi_{t}\mathcal{R}\|\,\|\Delta_{t}\|\cdot 2C_{\delta}r\|\Delta_{t}\|\,\mathbf{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>t\}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{equ:r_conditions}}}{{\leq}}\eta\lambda_{m}\|\Delta_{t}\|^{2}\,\mathbf{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>t\}},

(B.32)

where the second inequality also uses the fact that
0\preceq I-\varphi_{t}\mathcal{R}=(1-\varphi_{t})I+\varphi_{t}K^{\star}\preceq I
for
t
large enough.

Combining (
B.29
), (
B.31
) and (
B.32
) with (
B.3
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\|\Delta_{t+1}\|^{2}\mathbf{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>t\}}\big|\mathcal{F}_{t-1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq(1-\varphi_{t}\lambda_{m})^{2}\|\Delta_{t}\|^{2}\mathbf{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>t\}}+2\varphi_{t}^{2}(C_{q_{g},1}^{2/q_{g}}\|\Delta_{t}\|^{2}+C_{q_{g},2}^{2/q_{g}})

\displaystyle\quad+\varphi_{t}^{2}\cdot 2\eta\lambda_{m}\|\Delta_{t}\|^{2}\mathbf{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>t\}}+\varphi_{t}\cdot 2\eta\lambda_{m}\|\Delta_{t}\|^{2}\mathbf{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>t\}},

\displaystyle\implies u_{t+1}=\mathbb{E}\!\left[\|\Delta_{t+1}\|^{2}\mathbf{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>t+1\}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\!\left[\|\Delta_{t+1}\|^{2}\mathbf{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>t\}}\right]\leq(1-\varphi_{t}\lambda_{m})^{2}u_{t}+2\varphi_{t}^{2}(C_{q_{g},1}^{2/q_{g}}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Delta_{t}\|^{2}\right]+C_{q_{g},2}^{2/q_{g}})

\displaystyle\quad+\varphi_{t}^{2}\cdot 2\eta\lambda_{m}\,\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Delta_{t}\|^{2}\right]+\varphi_{t}\cdot 2\eta\lambda_{m}\,u_{t}

\displaystyle=\bigl[1-\varphi_{t}\cdot 2(1-\eta)\lambda_{m}\big]u_{t}+C_{u}\cdot\varphi_{t}^{2}.

(B.33)

Here, such a constant
C_{u}>0
exists since
u_{t}\leq\mathbb{E}[\|\Delta_{t}\|^{2}]\lesssim 1
as established in (
B.26
).

By
34
, we obtain for
\varphi\in(0.5,1]

\displaystyle\limsup_{t\rightarrow\infty}\frac{u_{t}}{\varphi_{t}}\ \lesssim\ \begin{cases}\frac{1}{2C_{\varphi}(1-\eta)\lambda_{m}}\cdot C_{u},&\varphi\in(0.5,1),\\
\frac{1}{2C_{\varphi}(1-\eta)\lambda_{m}-1}\cdot C_{u},&\varphi=1,\end{cases}\implies u_{t}=\mathbb{E}\!\left[\|\Delta_{t}\|^{2}\mathbf{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>t\}}\right]\lesssim\varphi_{t}.

(B.34)

Combining (
B.34
) with the second term on the right-hand side of (
B.3
), we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{2\Upsilon_{L}^{2}}{t^{2}}\left(\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{x}_{i}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>i\}}\right]\right)^{1/2}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{equ:indicator_x_bound}}}{{\lesssim}}\frac{1}{t^{2}}\left(\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\sqrt{\varphi_{i}}\right)^{2}=\frac{C_{\varphi}}{t^{2}}\left(\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\frac{1}{(i+1)^{\varphi/2}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{C_{\varphi}}{t^{2}}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{(x+1)^{\varphi/2}}\;dx\right)^{2}\leq\frac{C_{\varphi}}{(t+1)^{\varphi}}=\varphi_{t}.

(B.35)

Plugging (
B.3
) and (
B.3
) into (
B.3
) yields

\mathbb{E}[\|B_{t}-B^{\star}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>t\}}]\lesssim 1/t+\varphi_{t}\lesssim\varphi_{t},\quad\quad\varphi\in\left(0.5,1\right].

(B.36)

As discussed around (
B.3
), the above display implies

\|B_{t}-B^{\star}\|=O_{p}(\sqrt{\varphi_{t}}).

(B.37)

\bullet

Convergence rates of
(\alpha_{t},\beta_{t},\gamma_{t})
.

The result immediately follows from (
B.11
) and (
B.37
).

\bullet

Convergence rate of
K_{t}
.
The result immediately follows from (
B.14
) and (
B.37
).

This completes the proof of Lemma
4.2
.

B.4
Proof of Lemma
B.3

By Lemma
3.5
,
\bm{z}_{t,\tau}-(I-K_{t})\Delta\bm{x}_{t}
forms a martingale difference sequence with respect to filtration
\{\mathcal{F}_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
. Recalling that
B^{\star}=\nabla^{2}f(\bm{x}^{\star})
, we decompose the error
\Delta_{t+1}=\bm{x}_{t+1}-\bm{x}^{\star}
as follows

\displaystyle\Delta_{t+1}=\bm{x}_{t+1}-\bm{x}^{\star}=\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star}+\varphi_{t}\bm{z}_{t,\tau}

\displaystyle=\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star}+\varphi_{t}(I-K_{t})\Delta\bm{x}_{t}+\varphi_{t}(\bm{z}_{t,\tau}-(I-K_{t})\Delta\bm{x}_{t})

\displaystyle=\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star}-\varphi_{t}(I-K_{t})B_{t}^{-1}g_{t}+\varphi_{t}(\bm{z}_{t,\tau}-(I-K_{t})\Delta\bm{x}_{t})

\displaystyle=\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star}-\varphi_{t}(I-K_{t})B_{t}^{-1}\nabla f_{t}-\varphi_{t}(I-K_{t})B_{t}^{-1}(g_{t}-\nabla f_{t})+\varphi_{t}(\bm{z}_{t,\tau}-(I-K_{t})\Delta\bm{x}_{t})

\displaystyle=\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star}-\varphi_{t}(I-K_{t})(B^{\star})^{-1}\nabla f_{t}-\varphi_{t}(I-K_{t})[B_{t}^{-1}-(B^{\star})^{-1}]\nabla f_{t}+\varphi_{t}\bm{\theta}_{t}

\displaystyle=\{I-\varphi_{t}(I-K_{t})\}(\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star})-\varphi_{t}(I-K_{t})[(B^{\star})^{-1}(\nabla f_{t}-B^{\star}(\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star}))]-\varphi_{t}(I-K_{t})[B_{t}^{-1}-(B^{\star})^{-1}]\nabla f_{t}+\varphi_{t}\bm{\theta}_{t}

\displaystyle=\{I-\varphi_{t}(I-K^{\star})\}\Delta_{t}+\varphi_{t}(K_{t}-K^{\star})\Delta_{t}-\varphi_{t}(I-K_{t})[(B^{\star})^{-1}(\nabla f_{t}-B^{\star}\Delta_{t})]-\varphi_{t}(I-K_{t})[B_{t}^{-1}-(B^{\star})^{-1}]\nabla f_{t}+\varphi_{t}\bm{\theta}_{t}

\displaystyle=\{I-\varphi_{t}(I-K^{\star})\}\Delta_{t}+\varphi_{t}\bm{\delta}_{t}+\varphi_{t}\bm{\theta}_{t},

where

\bm{\theta}_{t}=-(I-K_{t})B_{t}^{-1}(g_{t}-\nabla f_{t})+(\bm{z}_{t,\tau}-(I-K_{t})\Delta\bm{x}_{t})

forms a martingale difference with respect to filtration
\{\mathcal{F}_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
, and

\bm{\delta}_{t}=(K_{t}-K^{\star})\Delta_{t}-(I-K_{t})[(B^{\star})^{-1}(\nabla f_{t}-B^{\star}\Delta_{t})]-(I-K_{t})[B_{t}^{-1}-(B^{\star})^{-1}]\nabla f_{t}

collects the higher-order approximation errors. This completes the proof of Lemma
B.3
.

B.5
Proof of Lemma
B.4

For
q_{g}>2
in Assumption
3.2
, we let
q=q_{g}
and have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{\theta}_{t}\|^{q}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\;\;\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{rec:def:b}}}}{{\leq}}\;\;2^{q-1}\left\{\mathbb{E}[\|(I-K_{t})B_{t}^{-1}(g_{t}-\nabla f_{t})\|^{q}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]+\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{z}_{t,\tau}-(I-K_{t})\Delta\bm{x}_{t}\|^{q}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\right\}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{q-1}\left(\mathbb{E}[\|(I-K_{t})B_{t}^{-1}\|^{q}\|g_{t}-\nabla f_{t}\|^{q}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]+\mathbb{E}[\|(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})\Delta\bm{x}_{t}\|^{q}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\right)\quad(\text{by Lemma \ref{lem:inner_transition}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{equ:uniform_bound_tK}}}}{{\leq}}2^{q-1}\left\{\left(\frac{3}{2\gamma_{H}}\right)^{q}\mathbb{E}[\|g_{t}-\nabla f_{t}\|^{q}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]+\left(C_{K}+\frac{1}{2}\right)^{q}\mathbb{E}[\|\Delta\bm{x}_{t}\|^{q}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\right\}\quad(\text{also }\|K_{t}\|\leq 0.5)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{q-1}\left\{\left(\frac{3}{2\gamma_{H}}\right)^{q}\left(C_{g,1}\|\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{q}+C_{g,2}\right)+\left(C_{H}+\frac{1}{2}\right)^{q}\mathbb{E}[\|B_{t}^{-1}g_{t}\|^{q}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\right\}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{ass:2:c2}}}}{{\leq}}\frac{1}{2\gamma_{H}^{q}}\left\{3^{q}\left(C_{g,1}\|\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{q}+C_{g,2}\right)+\left(2C_{H}+1\right)^{q}\mathbb{E}[\|g_{t}\|^{q}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2\gamma_{H}^{q}}\left\{3^{q}\left(C_{g,1}\|\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{q}+C_{g,2}\right)+\left(2C_{H}+1\right)^{q}\cdot 2^{q-1}(\|\nabla f_{t}\|^{q}+\mathbb{E}[\|g_{t}-\nabla f_{t}\|^{q}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}])\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2\gamma_{H}^{q}}\left\{3^{q}\left(C_{g,1}\|\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{q}+C_{g,2}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\left(4C_{H}+2\right)^{q}(\Upsilon_{H}^{q}\|\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{q}+C_{g,1}\|\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{q}+C_{g,2})\right\}

\displaystyle\eqqcolon C_{q,1}\|\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{q}+C_{q,2}.

Here, the second inequality holds due to Lemma
3.5
; and the fourth and the second last inequalities are both direct applications of Assumption
3.2
. This completes the proof.

B.6
Proof of Theorem
4.3

Let
\Phi_{t}\coloneqq\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\varphi_{i}
and
\lambda_{M}\coloneqq\lambda_{\max}(I-K^{\star})
.
By Lemma
4.1
, we have
K_{t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a.s.}}{{\rightarrow}}K^{\star}
.
Moreover, since
\|K^{\star}\|\leq\gamma_{H}/(2\Upsilon_{H})<0.5
, we know the matrix
\mathcal{R}=I-K^{\star}
is positive definite with
\lambda_{m}=\lambda_{\min}(\mathcal{R})>0.5
. Define

\Gamma_{t}\coloneqq\mathbb{E}\!\left[\bm{\theta}_{t+1}\bm{\theta}_{t+1}^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t}\right],\qquad\qquad\mathcal{A}_{t}\coloneqq\bigl\{\|\Gamma_{t}\|\leq 2\|\Gamma^{\star}\|\vee 1\bigr\}.

(B.38)

With these definitions, we can rewrite (
B.19
) as

\Delta_{t+1}=(I-\varphi_{t}\mathcal{R})\Delta_{t}+\varphi_{t}\bm{\theta}_{t}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{t-1}}+\varphi_{t}\bm{\delta}_{t}+\varphi_{t}\bm{\theta}_{t}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{t-1}^{c}}.

(B.39)

Applying (
B.39
) recursively gives us

\Delta_{t}=\prod_{i=0}^{t-1}(I-\varphi_{i}\mathcal{R})\Delta_{0}+\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\prod_{j=i+1}^{t-1}(I-\varphi_{j}\mathcal{R})\varphi_{i}[\bm{\theta}_{i}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}}+\bm{\delta}_{i}+\bm{\theta}_{i}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}^{c}}],

which implies

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varphi_{t-1}}}\Delta_{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varphi_{t-1}}}\Pi_{t,0}\Delta_{0}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varphi_{t-1}}}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\varphi_{i}\Pi_{t,i+1}\bm{\theta}_{i}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varphi_{t-1}}}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\varphi_{i}\Pi_{t,i+1}\bm{\delta}_{i}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varphi_{t-1}}}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\varphi_{i}\Pi_{t,i+1}\bm{\theta}_{i}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}^{c}}

\displaystyle\eqqcolon\mathcal{I}_{1,t}+\mathcal{I}_{2,t}+\mathcal{I}_{3,t}+\mathcal{I}_{4,t}.

(B.40)

Here, we define
\Pi_{n,m}\coloneqq\prod\limits_{i=m}^{n-1}(I-\varphi_{i}\mathcal{R})
for
m<n
and
I
otherwise. In what follows, we analyze each term on the right-hand side of (
B.6
) and establish the following claims:

(a)

\mathcal{I}_{1,t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a.s.}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbf{0}
,   as
t\rightarrow\infty
;

(b)

\mathcal{I}_{2,t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{0},\Sigma^{\star})
,   as
t\rightarrow\infty
,   where
\Sigma^{\star}
satisfies
(\mathcal{R}-\zeta I)\Sigma^{\star}+\Sigma^{\star}(\mathcal{R}-\zeta I)=\Gamma^{\star}
and
\zeta\coloneqq\mathbf{1}_{\{\varphi=1\}}/(2C_{\varphi})
;

(c)

\mathcal{I}_{3,t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbf{0}
,   as
t\rightarrow\infty
;

(d)

\mathcal{I}_{4,t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a.s.}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbf{0}
,   as
t\rightarrow\infty
.

By the four claims above, Slutsky’s theorem, and the fact that
\varphi_{t}/\varphi_{t-1}\rightarrow 1
, we immediately finish the proof of Theorem
4.3
.

\bullet

Proof of (a).
Since
\varphi_{t}\rightarrow 0
as
t\rightarrow\infty
, there exists
j\in\mathbb{N}
such that
1-\varphi_{i}\lambda_{M}>0,\,\forall\,i>j
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{I}_{1,t}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varphi_{t-1}}}\left\|\prod_{i=0}^{t-1}(I-\varphi_{i}\mathcal{R})\right\|\|\Delta_{0}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varphi_{t-1}}}\left\|\prod_{i=0}^{j}(I-\varphi_{i}\mathcal{R})\right\|\|\Delta_{0}\|\prod_{i=j+1}^{t-1}(1-\varphi_{i}\lambda_{m})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varphi_{t-1}}}\left\|\prod_{i=0}^{j}(I-\varphi_{i}\mathcal{R})\right\|\|\Delta_{0}\|\prod_{i=j+1}^{t-1}\exp(-\varphi_{i}\lambda_{m})

\displaystyle=\left\|\prod_{i=0}^{j}(I-\varphi_{i}\mathcal{R})\right\|\|\Delta_{0}\|\exp(\lambda_{m}\Phi_{j+1})\cdot\exp\left(-\lambda_{m}\Phi_{t}-0.5\log\varphi_{t-1}\right).

Note that
\left\|\prod_{i=0}^{j}(I-\varphi_{i}K)\right\|\|\Delta_{0}\|\exp(\lambda_{m}\Phi_{j+1})
is independent of
t
and let

d_{t}\coloneqq-\lambda_{m}\Phi_{t}-0.5\log\varphi_{t-1},

(B.41)

for which we have the following:

d_{t}\ \lesssim\ \begin{cases}-\,t^{\,1-\varphi}+\log t,&\varphi\in\left(\frac{1}{2},1\right),\\
-\left(\lambda_{m}C_{\varphi}-0.5\right)\log t,&\varphi=1.\end{cases}

Since
C_{\varphi}>1/(2\lambda_{m})
, we obtain
d_{t}\rightarrow-\infty
and
\mathcal{I}_{1,t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a.s.}}{{\rightarrow}}0
as
t\rightarrow\infty
.

\bullet

Proof of (b).
Recall that
\mathcal{I}_{2,t}=1/\sqrt{\varphi_{t-1}}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\varphi_{i}\Pi_{t,i+1}\bm{\theta}_{i}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}}
. Since
\mathcal{A}_{i-1}
is
\mathcal{F}_{i-1}
-measurable,
\mathcal{I}_{2,t}
is a sum of martingale increments and we can show its limiting distribution via the following central limit theorem for martingale arrays. For simplicity, we let

W_{t,i}=\frac{\varphi_{i}}{\sqrt{\varphi_{t-1}}}\Pi_{t,i+1}\bm{\theta}_{i}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}}.

Lemma B.5
.

(
25
, Corollary 3.1)

Let
\left(W_{t,i}\right)
be a triangular array of random vectors such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[W_{t,i}\middle|\mathcal{F}_{i-1}\right]=\mathbf{0},

(B.42)

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\mathbb{E}\left[W_{t,i}W_{t,i}^{\top}\middle|\mathcal{F}_{i-1}\right]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\rightarrow}}V^{*}\succeq\mathbf{0},

(B.43)

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\|W_{t,i}\|^{2}\mathbf{1}_{\{\|W_{t,i}\|>\epsilon\}}\middle|\mathcal{F}_{i-1}\right]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\rightarrow}}0,\quad\forall\,\epsilon>0,

(B.44)

then
\sum\limits_{i=0}^{t-1}W_{t,i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{0},V^{*})
as
t\rightarrow\infty
.

To proceed, we also need the following lemma.

Lemma B.6
.

Under the conditions of Theorem
4.3
, we have the following convergence as
t\rightarrow\infty

\Gamma_{t}=\mathbb{E}[\bm{\theta}_{t+1}\bm{\theta}_{t+1}^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a.s.}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathbb{E}[(I-\widetilde{K}^{\star})\Omega^{\star}(I-\widetilde{K}^{\star})^{\top}]=\Gamma^{\star}.

\bullet\bullet

Verifying (
B.42
).
By Lemma
3.5
, we obtain

\mathbb{E}[W_{t,i}|\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]=\frac{\varphi_{i}}{\sqrt{\varphi_{t-1}}}\Pi_{t,i+1}\mathbb{E}[\bm{\theta}_{i}|\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}}=\mathbf{0}.

\bullet\bullet

Verifying (
B.43
).

Let
\Sigma_{t}\coloneqq\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\mathbb{E}\left[W_{t,i}W_{t,i}^{\top}\middle|\mathcal{F}_{i-1}\right]
be the quadratic variation of
\mathcal{I}_{2,t}
. First, we show that
(\Sigma_{t})
is bounded as follows:

\displaystyle\|\Sigma_{t}\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\frac{\varphi_{i}^{2}}{\varphi_{t-1}}\Pi_{t,i+1}\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{\theta}_{i}\bm{\theta}_{i}^{T}\middle|\mathcal{F}_{i-1}\right]\Pi_{t,i+1}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\varphi_{t-1}}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\varphi_{i}^{2}\left\|\Pi_{t,i+1}\right\|^{2}\|\Gamma_{i-1}\|\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{equ:At}}}{{\leq}}\frac{1}{\varphi_{t-1}}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\varphi_{i}^{2}\left\|\Pi_{t,i+1}\right\|^{2}\cdot(2\|\Gamma^{\star}\|\vee 1).

Similar to the argument in the proof of
(a)
, there exists
j\in\mathbb{N}
such that
1-\varphi_{i}\lambda_{M}>0,\,\forall\,i>j
. Therefore, for
t
large enough, we have

\|\Sigma_{t}\|\lesssim\underbrace{\frac{1}{\varphi_{t-1}}\sum_{i=0}^{j}\varphi_{i}^{2}\left\|\Pi_{j+1,i+1}\right\|^{2}\cdot\prod_{k=j+1}^{t-1}(1-\varphi_{k}\lambda_{m})^{2}}_{a_{t}}+\underbrace{\frac{1}{\varphi_{t-1}}\sum_{i=j+1}^{t-1}\varphi_{i}^{2}\prod_{k=i+1}^{t-1}(1-\varphi_{k}\lambda_{m})^{2}}_{b_{t}}.

Then we analyze the two sequences
(a_{t})
and
(b_{t})
separately. For
(a_{t})
, we have

a_{t}=\frac{1}{\varphi_{t-1}}\sum_{i=0}^{j}\varphi_{i}^{2}\left\|\Pi_{j+1,i+1}\right\|^{2}\cdot\prod_{k=j+1}^{t-1}(1-\varphi_{k}\lambda_{m})^{2}\\
\leq\frac{1}{\varphi_{t-1}}\left(\sum_{i=0}^{j}\varphi_{i}^{2}\right)\left(\max_{0\leq i\leq j}\left\|\Pi_{j+1,i+1}\right\|^{2}\right)\cdot\prod_{k=j+1}^{t-1}(1-\varphi_{k}\lambda_{m})^{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{\varphi_{t-1}}\prod_{k=j+1}^{t-1}\exp(-2\varphi_{k}\lambda_{m})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{def:d_t}}}{{\lesssim}}\exp(2d_{t}).

As shown in the proof of
(a)
,
d_{t}\rightarrow-\infty
as
t\rightarrow\infty
. Thus,
a_{t}\rightarrow 0
as
t\rightarrow\infty
. For
(b_{t})
, we have the following recursive relation:

\displaystyle b_{t}=\frac{1}{\varphi_{t-1}}\sum_{i=j+1}^{t-1}\varphi_{i}^{2}\prod_{k=i+1}^{t-1}(1-\varphi_{k}\lambda_{m})^{2}=\frac{1}{\varphi_{t-1}}\left[\varphi_{t-1}^{2}+\sum_{i=j+1}^{t-2}\varphi_{i}^{2}\prod_{k=i+1}^{t-2}(1-\varphi_{k}\lambda_{m})^{2}\cdot(1-\varphi_{t-1}\lambda_{m})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\implies\varphi_{t-1}b_{t}=(1-\varphi_{t-1}\lambda_{m})^{2}\varphi_{t-2}b_{t-1}+\varphi_{t-1}^{2}.

By
34
, we directly obtain

\limsup_{t\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\varphi_{t-1}b_{t}}{1/t^{\varphi}}\ \leq\ \begin{cases}\frac{1}{2C_{\varphi}\lambda_{m}}\cdot C_{\varphi}^{2},&\varphi\in(0.5,1),\\
\frac{1}{2C_{\varphi}\lambda_{m}-1}\cdot C_{\varphi}^{2},&\varphi=1,\end{cases}\quad\implies\quad\limsup_{t\rightarrow\infty}b_{t}\ \leq\ \begin{cases}\frac{1}{2\lambda_{m}},&\varphi\in(0.5,1),\\
\frac{C_{\varphi}}{2C_{\varphi}\lambda_{m}-1},&\varphi=1.\end{cases}

Thus,
(b_{t})
is bounded for
\varphi\in(0.5,1]
. Combining the above four displays, we obtain that
\|\Sigma_{t}\|
is bounded by a deterministic upper bound almost surely.
Furthermore, by the definition of
\Sigma_{t}
, we have the following recursive relation:

\displaystyle\varphi_{t-1}\Sigma_{t}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\varphi_{i}^{2}\Pi_{t,i+1}\Gamma_{i-1}\Pi_{t,i+1}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}}

\displaystyle=\varphi_{t-1}^{2}\Gamma_{t-2}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{t-2}}+(I-\varphi_{t-1}\mathcal{R})\left(\sum_{i=0}^{t-2}\varphi_{i}^{2}\Pi_{t,i+1}\Gamma_{i-1}\Pi_{t,i+1}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}}\right)(I-\varphi_{t-1}\mathcal{R})

\displaystyle=\varphi_{t-1}^{2}\Gamma_{t-2}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{t-2}}+\varphi_{t-2}(I-\varphi_{t-1}\mathcal{R})\Sigma_{t-1}(I-\varphi_{t-1}\mathcal{R})

\displaystyle=\varphi_{t-1}^{2}\Gamma_{t-2}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{t-2}}+\varphi_{t-2}\left[\Sigma_{t-1}-\varphi_{t-1}\mathcal{R}\Sigma_{t-1}-\varphi_{t-1}\Sigma_{t-1}\mathcal{R}+O\left(\varphi_{t-1}^{2}\right)\right],

where the last equality uses the result that
(\Sigma_{t})
is deterministically bounded as we have shown earlier.
Dividing by
\varphi_{t-1}
on both sides, we obtain

\Sigma_{t}=\Sigma_{t-1}-\varphi_{t-1}\left(\mathcal{R}\Sigma_{t-1}+\Sigma_{t-1}\mathcal{R}-\Gamma_{t-2}\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{A}_{t-2}}}\right)+\frac{\varphi_{t-2}-\varphi_{t-1}}{\varphi_{t-1}}\Sigma_{t-1}+O\left(\varphi_{t-2}\varphi_{t-1}+|\varphi_{t-2}-\varphi_{t-1}|\right),

which gives us the following:

\Sigma_{t}=\begin{cases}\Sigma_{t-1}-\varphi_{t-1}\left(\mathcal{R}\Sigma_{t-1}+\Sigma_{t-1}\mathcal{R}-\Gamma_{t-2}\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{A}_{t-2}}}\right)+o(\varphi_{t-1}),&\varphi\in(0.5,1),\\
\Sigma_{t-1}-\varphi_{t-1}\left[\left(\mathcal{R}-\frac{I}{2C_{\varphi}}\right)\Sigma_{t-1}+\Sigma_{t-1}\left(\mathcal{R}-\frac{I}{2C_{\varphi}}\right)-\Gamma_{t-2}\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{A}_{t-2}}}\right]+o(\varphi_{t-1}),&\varphi=1.\end{cases}

Recall that
\zeta=\mathbf{1}_{\{\varphi=1\}}/(2C_{\varphi})
and define
\mathcal{R}_{\zeta}\coloneqq\mathcal{R}-\zeta I
. We can combine the above two cases for
\varphi\in(0.5,1]
as

\Sigma_{t}=\Sigma_{t-1}-\varphi_{t-1}\left(\mathcal{R}_{\zeta}\Sigma_{t-1}+\Sigma_{t-1}\mathcal{R}_{\zeta}-\Gamma_{t-2}\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{A}_{t-2}}}\right)+o(\varphi_{t-1}).

Then, we vectorize this equation by letting
s_{t}=\text{vec}(\Sigma_{t})
, and obtain

\displaystyle s_{t}

\displaystyle=s_{t-1}-\varphi_{t-1}\left[\text{vec}(\mathcal{R}_{\zeta}\Sigma_{t-1}+\Sigma_{t-1}\mathcal{R}_{\zeta})-\text{vec}(\Gamma_{t-2}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{t-2}})\right]+o(\varphi_{t-1})

\displaystyle=s_{t-1}-\varphi_{t-1}\left[(I\otimes\mathcal{R}_{\zeta}+\mathcal{R}_{\zeta}\otimes I)\,s_{t-1}-\text{vec}(\Gamma^{\star})\right]+\varphi_{t-1}\text{vec}(\Gamma_{t-2}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{t-2}}-\Gamma^{\star})+o(\varphi_{t-1})

\displaystyle=s_{t-1}-\varphi_{t-1}(Q\,s_{t-1}-\text{vec}(\Gamma^{\star}))+\varepsilon_{t-1}\varphi_{t-1},

where
Q=I\otimes\mathcal{R}_{\zeta}+\mathcal{R}_{\zeta}\otimes I
and
\varepsilon_{t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a.s.}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathbf{0}
, which is due to
\Gamma_{t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a.s.}}{{\rightarrow}}\Gamma^{\star}
given in Lemma
B.6
.
Let
s^{\star}
be the solution of equation
Qs-\text{vec}(\Gamma^{\star})=\mathbf{0}
. The existence and uniqueness of such a solution are guaranteed since
\mathcal{R}_{\zeta}=\mathcal{R}-\zeta I
is positive definite. Since all eigenvalues of
\mathcal{R}_{\zeta}
are strictly positive, the sum of any pair of eigenvalues is non-zero, implying that
Q
is positive definite. For
t
large enough, we have
1-2\varphi_{t-1}\lambda_{M}>0
and hence
\|I-\varphi_{t-1}Q\|=1-2\varphi_{t-1}\lambda_{m}
. Then, we can rewrite the recursive relation as

s_{t}-s^{\star}=(s_{t-1}-s^{\star})-\varphi_{t-1}Q(s_{t-1}-s^{\star})+\varepsilon_{t-1}\varphi_{t-1}=(I-\varphi_{t-1}Q)(s_{t-1}-s^{\star})+\varepsilon_{t-1}\varphi_{t-1},

which implies

\|s_{t}-s^{\star}\|\leq\|I-\varphi_{t-1}Q\|\|s_{t-1}-s^{\star}\|+\|\varepsilon_{t-1}\|\varphi_{t-1}=(1-2\varphi_{t-1}\lambda_{m})\|s_{t-1}-s^{\star}\|+\|\varepsilon_{t-1}\|\varphi_{t-1}.

By
34
, for
\varphi\in(0.5,1]
, almost surely we have

\limsup_{t\rightarrow\infty}\|s_{t}-s^{\star}\|\leq\frac{1}{2C_{\varphi}\lambda_{m}}\cdot 0,\quad\implies\quad\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\|s_{t}-s^{\star}\|=0,

which is equivalent to
\Sigma_{t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a.s.}}{{\rightarrow}}\Sigma^{*}
, where
\Sigma^{*}
is the unique solution of the Lyapunov equation

(\mathcal{R}-\zeta I)\Sigma^{\star}+\Sigma^{\star}(\mathcal{R}-\zeta I)=\Gamma^{\star}.

\bullet\bullet

Verifying (
B.44
).
For simplicity, let

\sigma_{t}^{2}\coloneqq\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\|W_{t,i}\|^{2}\mathbf{1}_{\{\|W_{t,i}\|>\epsilon\}}\middle|\mathcal{F}_{i-1}\right]

for any given
\epsilon>0
. Thus, it suffices to show that
\sigma_{t}^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a.s.}}{{\rightarrow}}0
as
t\rightarrow\infty
. Under Assumption
3.2
with
q_{g}>2
, we write
q_{g}=2+\delta
for some
\delta>0
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\sigma_{t}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\frac{\varphi_{i}^{2}}{\varphi_{t-1}}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Pi_{t,i+1}\|^{2}\mathbf{1}_{\{\varphi_{i}\|\Pi_{t,i+1}\bm{\theta}_{i}\|>\epsilon\sqrt{\varphi_{t-1}}\}}\middle|\mathcal{F}_{i-1}\right]\leq\frac{1}{\epsilon^{\delta}}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\frac{\varphi_{i}^{2+\delta}}{\varphi_{t-1}^{1+\delta/2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Pi_{t,i+1}\bm{\theta}_{i}\|^{2+\delta}\middle|\mathcal{F}_{i-1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\epsilon^{\delta}}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\left(\frac{\varphi_{i}}{\sqrt{\varphi_{t-1}}}\|\Pi_{t,i+1}\|\right)^{2+\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{\theta}_{i}\|^{2+\delta}\middle|\mathcal{F}_{i-1}\right].

(B.45)

By Lemma
B.4
, there exist
C_{2+\delta,1},C_{2+\delta,2}>0
such that

\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{\theta}_{t}\|^{2+\delta}\middle|\mathcal{F}_{t-1}\right]\leq C_{2+\delta,1}\|\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{2+\delta}+C_{2+\delta,2}.

Since
\bm{x}_{t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a.s.}}{{\rightarrow}}\bm{x}^{*}
as
t\rightarrow\infty
, we can conclude that the sequence
\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{\theta}_{t}\|^{2+\delta}\middle|\mathcal{F}_{t-1}\right]\right)
is bounded almost surely. Therefore, in order to show
\sigma_{t}^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a.s.}}{{\rightarrow}}0
as
t\rightarrow\infty
, we only need to show

S_{t}\coloneqq\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\left(\frac{\varphi_{i}}{\sqrt{\varphi_{t-1}}}\|\Pi_{t,i+1}\|\right)^{2+\delta}\rightarrow 0\quad\text{as \,}t\rightarrow\infty.

Again, similar to the argument in the proof of
(a)
, there exists
j\in\mathbb{N}
such that
1-\varphi_{i}\lambda_{M}>0,\,\forall\,i>j
. Therefore, for
t
large enough, we have

S_{t}\leq\underbrace{\frac{1}{\varphi_{t-1}^{1+\delta/2}}\sum_{i=0}^{j}\varphi_{i}^{2+\delta}\left\|\Pi_{j+1,i+1}\right\|^{2+\delta}\cdot\prod_{k=j+1}^{t-1}(1-\varphi_{k}\lambda_{m})^{2+\delta}}_{a_{t}^{\prime}}+\underbrace{\frac{1}{\varphi_{t-1}^{1+\delta/2}}\sum_{i=j+1}^{t-1}\varphi_{i}^{2+\delta}\prod_{k=i+1}^{t-1}(1-\varphi_{k}\lambda_{m})^{2+\delta}}_{b_{t}^{\prime}}.

We analyze the two sequences
(a_{t}^{\prime})
and
(b_{t}^{\prime})
separately. For
(a_{t}^{\prime})
, we have

\displaystyle a_{t}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\varphi_{t-1}^{1+\delta/2}}\sum_{i=0}^{j}\varphi_{i}^{2+\delta}\left\|\Pi_{j+1,i+1}\right\|^{2+\delta}\cdot\prod_{k=j+1}^{t-1}(1-\varphi_{k}\lambda_{m})^{2+\delta}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\varphi_{t-1}^{1+\delta/2}}\left(\sum_{i=0}^{j}\varphi_{i}^{2+\delta}\right)\left(\max_{0\leq i\leq j}\left\|\Pi_{j+1,i+1}\right\|^{2+\delta}\right)\cdot\prod_{k=j+1}^{t-1}(1-\varphi_{k}\lambda_{m})^{2+\delta}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\varphi_{t-1}^{1+\delta/2}}\prod_{k=j+1}^{t-1}\exp(-(2+\delta)\varphi_{k}\lambda_{m})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{def:d_t}}}{{\lesssim}}\exp((2+\delta)d_{t}).

As discussed in the proof of
(a)
,
d_{t}\rightarrow-\infty
as
t\rightarrow\infty
. Thus,
a_{t}^{\prime}\rightarrow 0
as
t\rightarrow\infty
. For
(b_{t}^{\prime})
, we have the following recursive relation:

\displaystyle b_{t}^{\prime}=\frac{1}{\varphi_{t-1}^{1+\delta/2}}\sum_{i=j+1}^{t-1}\varphi_{i}^{2+\delta}\prod_{k=i+1}^{t-1}(1-\varphi_{k}\lambda_{m})^{2+\delta}=\frac{1}{\varphi_{t-1}^{1+\delta/2}}\left[\varphi_{t-1}^{2+\delta}+\sum_{i=j+1}^{t-2}\varphi_{i}^{2+\delta}\prod_{k=i+1}^{t-2}(1-\varphi_{k}\lambda_{m})^{2+\delta}\cdot(1-\varphi_{t-1}\lambda_{m})^{2+\delta}\right]

\displaystyle\implies\varphi_{t-1}^{1+\delta/2}b_{t}^{\prime}=(1-\varphi_{t-1}\lambda_{m})^{2+\delta}\varphi_{t-2}^{1+\delta/2}b_{t-1}^{\prime}+\varphi_{t-1}^{2+\delta}.

By
34
, we directly obtain

\limsup_{t\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\varphi_{t-1}^{1+\delta/2}b_{t}^{\prime}}{(1/t^{\varphi})^{1+\delta/2}}\ \leq\ \begin{cases}\frac{1}{(2+\delta)C_{\varphi}\lambda_{m}}\limsup_{t\rightarrow\infty}(\frac{1}{t^{\varphi}})^{\delta/2},&\varphi\in(0.5,1),\\
\frac{1}{(2+\delta)C_{\varphi}\lambda_{m}-(1+\delta/2)}\limsup_{t\rightarrow\infty}(\frac{1}{t^{\varphi}})^{\delta/2},&\varphi=1,\end{cases}\quad\implies\quad\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}b_{t}^{\prime}=0.

Combining the above four displays, we can conclude that
S_{t}\rightarrow 0
and hence
\sigma_{t}^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a.s.}}{{\rightarrow}}0
as
t\rightarrow\infty
by (
B.6
). Therefore, all the conditions of Lemma
B.5
are satisfied, and we conclude that

\mathcal{I}_{2,t}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varphi_{t-1}}}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\varphi_{i}\Pi_{t,i+1}\bm{\theta}_{i}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}}\xrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{0},\Sigma^{*}),

where
\Sigma^{*}
satisfies
(\mathcal{R}-\zeta I)\Sigma^{*}+\Sigma^{*}(\mathcal{R}-\zeta I)=\Gamma^{\star}
.

\bullet

Proof of (c).
By arguments analogous to (
B.3
), it suffices to show that

\mathbb{E}[\|\mathcal{I}_{3,t}\|\bm{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>t\}}]=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varphi_{t}}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\varphi_{i}\Pi_{t,i+1}\bm{\delta}_{i}\right\|\bm{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>t\}}\right]\rightarrow 0\quad\text{as }\,t\rightarrow\infty.

Here, the stopping time
\tau_{k,r}
is defined as in Section
B.3
, with the same choice of
\eta
and the corresponding parameters
r
and
k
. For simplicity, let

e_{t}\coloneqq\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\varphi_{i}\Pi_{t,i+1}\bm{\delta}_{i}\right\|\bm{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>t\}}\right].

For all sufficiently large
t
(in particular, for
t\geq k
), the sequence
\{e_{t}\}
satisfies the following recursion:

\displaystyle e_{t}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\varphi_{t-1}\bm{\delta}_{t-1}\right\|\bm{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>t\}}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left(I-\varphi_{t-1}\mathcal{R}\right)\left(\sum_{i=0}^{t-2}\varphi_{i}\Pi_{t-1,i+1}\bm{\delta}_{i}\right)\right\|\bm{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>t\}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\varphi_{t-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\bm{\delta}_{t-1}\right\|\bm{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>t-1\}}\right]+(1-\varphi_{t-1}\lambda_{m})\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sum_{i=0}^{t-2}\varphi_{i}\Pi_{t-1,i+1}\bm{\delta}_{i}\right\|\bm{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>t-1\}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq(1-\varphi_{t-1}\lambda_{m})e_{t-1}+\varphi_{t-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{\delta}_{t-1}\|\bm{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>t-1\}}\right].

(B.46)

To characterize the asymptotic behavior of
e_{t}
, it remains to bound
\mathbb{E}\!\left[\|\bm{\delta}_{t}\|\mathbf{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>t\}}\right]
. By (
B.22
) and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{\delta}_{t}\|\bm{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>t\}}]

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\|B_{t}-B^{\star}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>t\}}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Delta_{t}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>t\}}\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}\eqref{equ:B_t_local_rate}\\
\eqref{equ:indicator_x_bound}\end{subarray}}}}{{\lesssim}}\;\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}(H_{i}-\nabla^{2}f_{i})\right\|^{2}\right]+\frac{1}{t^{2}}\left(\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{x}_{i}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>i\}}\right]\right)^{1/2}\right)^{2}+\varphi_{t}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}\eqref{equ:B_t_local_rate_term1}\\
\eqref{equ:B_t_local_rate_term2}\end{subarray}}}}{{\lesssim}}\;\frac{1}{t}+\varphi_{t}+\varphi_{t}\lesssim\varphi_{t}.

Therefore, the recursive relation in (
B.6
) can be written as

e_{t}\leq(1-\varphi_{t-1}\lambda_{m})e_{t-1}+\varphi_{t-1}\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{\delta}_{t}\|\bm{1}_{\{\tau_{k,r}>t\}}]\leq(1-\varphi_{t-1}\lambda_{m})e_{t-1}+O(\varphi_{t-1}^{2}),

which implies

\displaystyle\frac{e_{t}}{\sqrt{\varphi_{t-1}}}\leq(1-\varphi_{t-1}\lambda_{m})\frac{e_{t-1}}{\sqrt{\varphi_{t-2}}}\sqrt{\frac{\varphi_{t-2}}{\varphi_{t-1}}}+\frac{O(\varphi_{t-1}^{2})}{\sqrt{\varphi_{t-1}}}\leq(1-\varphi_{t-1}\lambda_{m})\left(1+\frac{\varphi}{2t}+O(1/t^{2})\right)\frac{e_{t-1}}{\sqrt{\varphi_{t-2}}}+O(\varphi_{t-1}^{3/2}).

\bullet\bullet

Case 1:
\varphi\in(0.5,1)
.
Given that
1/t=o(\varphi_{t-1})
, there exists
\eta_{1}\in(0,1)
such that

(1-\varphi_{t-1}\lambda_{m})\left(1+\frac{\varphi}{2t}+O(1/t^{2})\right)\leq 1-\varphi_{t-1}\lambda_{m}(1-\eta_{1})

for all
t
large enough. Therefore, we have

\frac{e_{t}}{\sqrt{\varphi_{t-1}}}\leq(1-\varphi_{t-1}\lambda_{m}(1-\eta_{1}))\frac{e_{t-1}}{\sqrt{\varphi_{t-2}}}+O(\varphi_{t-1}^{3/2}).

By
34
, we obtain

\limsup_{t\rightarrow\infty}\frac{e_{t}}{\sqrt{\varphi_{t-1}}}\lesssim\frac{1}{C_{\varphi}\lambda_{m}(1-\eta_{1})}\cdot\limsup_{t\rightarrow\infty}\sqrt{\varphi_{t-1}},\quad\implies\quad\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\frac{e_{t}}{\sqrt{\varphi_{t-1}}}=0.

\bullet\bullet

Case 2:
\varphi=1
.
Given
C_{\varphi}>1/(2\lambda_{m})
, we have

(1-\varphi_{t-1}\lambda_{m})\left(1+\frac{1}{2t}+O(1/t^{2})\right)=1-\frac{1}{t}\left(C_{\varphi}\lambda_{m}-\frac{1}{2}\right)+O(1/t^{2}).

There exists
\eta_{2}\in(0,1)
such that

(1-\varphi_{t-1}\lambda_{m})\left(1+\frac{\varphi}{2t}+O(1/t^{2})\right)\leq 1-\frac{1}{t}\left(C_{\varphi}\lambda_{m}-\frac{1}{2}\right)(1-\eta_{2})

for all
t
large enough. Therefore, we have the following recursion:

\frac{e_{t}}{\sqrt{\varphi_{t-1}}}\leq\left(1-\frac{1}{t}\left(C_{\varphi}\lambda_{m}-\frac{1}{2}\right)(1-\eta_{2})\right)\frac{e_{t-1}}{\sqrt{\varphi_{t-2}}}+O(\varphi_{t-1}^{3/2}).

Again by
34
, we obtain

\limsup_{t\rightarrow\infty}\frac{e_{t}}{\sqrt{\varphi_{t-1}}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\left(C_{\varphi}\lambda_{m}-\frac{1}{2}\right)(1-\eta_{2})}\cdot\limsup_{t\rightarrow\infty}\sqrt{\varphi_{t-1}},\quad\implies\quad\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\frac{e_{t}}{\sqrt{\varphi_{t-1}}}=0.

Combining
Case 1
and
Case 2
, we conclude that
\mathcal{I}_{3,t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathbf{0}
as
t\rightarrow\infty
.

\bullet

Proof of (d).
By Lemma
B.6
, we know for each run of the algorithm, there exists an (potentially random) index
J<\infty
such that
\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}^{c}}=0
for all
i>J
. Thus, for such a realization, we have for
t>J
that

\bigl\|\mathcal{I}_{4,t}\bigr\|\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varphi_{t-1}}}\sum_{i=0}^{J}\varphi_{i}\,\|\Pi_{t,i+1}\|\|\bm{\theta}_{i}\|\lesssim\left(\sum_{i=0}^{J}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varphi_{t-1}}}\|\Pi_{t,i+1}\|\right)\left(\max_{0\leq i\leq J}\|\bm{\theta}_{i}\|\right).

As in the proof of
(a)
, for any
0\leq i\leq J
, we have
\|\Pi_{t,i+1}\|/\sqrt{\varphi_{t-1}}\rightarrow 0
as
t\rightarrow\infty
. This concludes that
\mathcal{I}_{4,t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a.s.}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathbf{0}
as
t\rightarrow\infty
.

Now, we have proved
(a)-(d)
and, by (
B.6
), we complete the proof of Theorem
4.3
.

B.7
Proof of Lemma
B.6

Recall from (
B.20a
) that
\bm{\theta}_{t}=-(I-K_{t})B_{t}^{-1}(g_{t}-\nabla f_{t})+(\bm{z}_{t,\tau}-(I-K_{t})\Delta\bm{x}_{t})
forms a martingale difference sequence. We now investigate the limiting covariance of
\mathbb{E}[\bm{\theta}_{t}\bm{\theta}_{t}^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]
. Note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\bm{\theta}_{t}\bm{\theta}_{t}^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\{(I-K_{t})B_{t}^{-1}(g_{t}-\nabla f_{t})-(\bm{z}_{t,\tau}-(I-K_{t})\Delta\bm{x}_{t})\}

\displaystyle\qquad\{(I-K_{t})B_{t}^{-1}(g_{t}-\nabla f_{t})-(\bm{z}_{t,\tau}-(I-K_{t})\Delta\bm{x}_{t})\}^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]

\displaystyle=(I-K_{t})B_{t}^{-1}\mathbb{E}[(g_{t}-\nabla f_{t})(g_{t}-\nabla f_{t})^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]B_{t}^{-1}(I-K_{t})

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}[(\bm{z}_{t,\tau}-(I-K_{t})\Delta\bm{x}_{t})(\bm{z}_{t,\tau}-(I-K_{t})\Delta\bm{x}_{t})^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\coloneqq\mathcal{J}_{1,t}+\mathcal{J}_{2,t},

(B.47)

where the second equality is due to the tower property and the fact that
\bm{z}_{t,\tau}-(I-K_{t})\Delta\bm{x}_{t}
forms a martingale difference sequence given in Lemma
3.5
. Specifically,

\mathbb{E}[(g_{t}-\nabla f_{t})(\bm{z}_{t,\tau}-(I-K_{t})\Delta\bm{x}_{t})^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]=\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[(g_{t}-\nabla f_{t})(\bm{z}_{t,\tau}-(I-K_{t})\Delta\bm{x}_{t})^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-0.5}]\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\\
=\mathbb{E}[(g_{t}-\nabla f_{t})\mathbb{E}[(\bm{z}_{t,\tau}-(I-K_{t})\Delta\bm{x}_{t})^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-0.5}]\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]=\bm{0}.

\bullet

Convergence of the
\mathcal{J}_{1,t}
.

For the term
\mathcal{J}_{1,t}
, we have
\mathbb{E}[(g_{t}-\nabla f_{t})(g_{t}-\nabla f_{t})^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]=\mathbb{E}[g_{t}g^{\top}_{t}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]-\nabla f_{t}\nabla f_{t}^{\top}
. Also note that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{E}[g_{t}g^{\top}_{t}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]-\mathbb{E}[\nabla F(\bm{x}^{\star};\xi)(\nabla F(\bm{x}^{\star};\xi))^{\top}]\right\|=\left\|\mathbb{E}[g_{t}g^{\top}_{t}-\nabla F(\bm{x}^{\star};\xi_{t})(\nabla F(\bm{x}^{\star};\xi_{t}))^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}[\|g_{t}-\nabla F(\bm{x}^{\star};\xi_{t})\|\cdot\|g_{t}\|\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]+\mathbb{E}[\|g_{t}-\nabla F(\bm{x}^{\star};\xi_{t})\|^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]

\displaystyle\leq 2\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[\|g_{t}-\nabla F(\bm{x}^{\star};\xi_{t})\|^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[\|g_{t}\|^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]}+\mathbb{E}[\|g_{t}-\nabla F(\bm{x}^{\star};\xi_{t})\|^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}],

(B.48)

where the first equality holds since
\xi_{t}
is independent of
\mathcal{F}_{t-1}
, the second inequality uses
\|aa^{\top}-bb^{\top}\|\leq 2\|a\|\|a-b\|+\|a-b\|^{2}
and the last inequality follows from the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality. For the first term in (
B.7
), by the
\Upsilon_{H}
-Lipschitz continuity of
\nabla f(\bm{x})
and Assumption
3.2
with
q_{g}>2
, we have

\mathbb{E}[\|g_{t}-\nabla F(\bm{x}^{\star};\xi_{t})\|^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\leq\mathbb{E}[\sup_{\bm{x}}\|\nabla^{2}F(\bm{x};\xi)\|^{2}]\cdot\|\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{2}\leq\Upsilon_{H}^{2}\|\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{2},

(B.49)

and

\displaystyle\hskip-8.5359pt\mathbb{E}[\|g_{t}\|^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\leq 2\|\nabla f_{t}\|^{2}+2\mathbb{E}[\|g_{t}-\nabla f_{t}\|^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\leq 2\Upsilon_{H}^{2}\|\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{2}+2C_{g,1}^{2/q_{g}}\|\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{2}+2C_{q_{g},2}^{2/q_{g}}.

(B.50)

Here the second inequality in (
B.50
) also uses
2/q_{g}\in(0,1)
and hence
(a+b)^{2/q_{g}}\leq a^{2/q_{g}}+b^{2/q_{g}}
for
a,b\geq 0
. Plugging (
B.49
) and (
B.50
) into (
B.7
) gives us

\left\|\mathbb{E}[g_{t}g^{\top}_{t}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]-\mathbb{E}[\nabla F(\bm{x}^{\star};\xi_{t})(\nabla F(\bm{x}^{\star};\xi_{t}))^{\top}]\right\|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a.s.}}{{\rightarrow}}0,

which further implies

\mathbb{E}[(g_{t}-\nabla f_{t})(g_{t}-\nabla f_{t})^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a.s.}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathbb{E}[\nabla F(\bm{x}^{\star};\xi)(\nabla F(\bm{x}^{\star};\xi))^{\top}].

(B.51)

Since
K_{t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a.s.}}{{\rightarrow}}K^{\star}
and
B_{t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a.s.}}{{\rightarrow}}B^{\star}
, which are shown in Lemma
4.1
, we then have

\mathcal{J}_{1,t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a.s.}}{{\rightarrow}}(I-K^{\star})(B^{\star})^{-1}\mathbb{E}[\nabla F(\bm{x}^{\star};\xi)(\nabla F(\bm{x}^{\star};\xi))^{\top}](B^{\star})^{-1}(I-K^{\star})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{equ:Gamma_star}}}{{=}}(I-K^{\star})\Omega^{\star}(I-K^{\star}).

(B.52)

\bullet

Convergence of the
\mathcal{J}_{2,t}
.

For the term
\mathcal{J}_{2,t}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{2,t}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[(\bm{z}_{t,\tau}-(I-K_{t})\Delta\bm{x}_{t})(\bm{z}_{t,\tau}-(I-K_{t})\Delta\bm{x}_{t})^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})\Delta\bm{x}_{t}\Delta\bm{x}_{t}^{\top}(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})B_{t}^{-1}g_{t}g^{\top}_{t}B_{t}^{-1}(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})B_{t}^{-1}(g_{t}-\nabla f_{t})(g_{t}-\nabla f_{t})^{\top}B_{t}^{-1}(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})B_{t}^{-1}\nabla f_{t}\nabla f_{t}^{\top}B_{t}^{-1}(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})B_{t}^{-1}(g_{t}-\nabla f_{t})\nabla f_{t}^{\top}B_{t}^{-1}(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})B_{t}^{-1}\nabla f_{t}(g_{t}-\nabla f_{t})^{\top}B_{t}^{-1}(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}].

(B.53)

For the last two terms in (
B.7
), we can apply the tower property. In particular, for the last term, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})B_{t}^{-1}\nabla f_{t}(g_{t}-\nabla f_{t})^{\top}B_{t}^{-1}(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})B_{t}^{-1}\nabla f_{t}\mathbb{E}[(g_{t}-\nabla f_{t})^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}\cup\sigma(\{S_{t,j}\}_{j})]B_{t}^{-1}(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})B_{t}^{-1}\nabla f_{t}\mathbb{E}[(g_{t}-\nabla f_{t})^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]B_{t}^{-1}(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]=\bm{0},

(B.54)

where the last equality holds since
\mathbb{E}[g_{t}-\nabla f_{t}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]=0
. Similarly, for the third term in (
B.7
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})B_{t}^{-1}(g_{t}-\nabla f_{t})\nabla f_{t}^{\top}B_{t}^{-1}(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})B_{t}^{-1}\mathbb{E}[(g_{t}-\nabla f_{t})\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}\cup\sigma(\{S_{t,j}\}_{j})]\nabla f_{t}^{\top}B_{t}^{-1}(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})B_{t}^{-1}\mathbb{E}[(g_{t}-\nabla f_{t})\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\nabla f_{t}^{\top}B_{t}^{-1}(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]=\bm{0}.

(B.55)

For the second term in (
B.7
), we have

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})B_{t}^{-1}\nabla f_{t}\nabla f_{t}^{\top}B_{t}^{-1}(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\right\|\leq\mathbb{E}[\|(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})B_{t}^{-1}\nabla f_{t}\nabla f_{t}^{\top}B_{t}^{-1}(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})^{\top}\|\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}[\|(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})\|^{2}\|B_{t}^{-1}\|^{2}\|\nabla f_{t}\|^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\leq(C_{K}+0.5)^{2}\cdot 1/\gamma_{H}^{2}\cdot\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla f_{t}\|^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]

\displaystyle=(C_{K}+0.5)^{2}\cdot 1/\gamma_{H}^{2}\cdot\|\nabla f_{t}\|^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a.s.}}{{\rightarrow}}0.

(B.56)

Here, the third inequality follows from the uniform bounds

\|\widetilde{K}_{t}\|\leq C_{K}
in (
A.5
),

\|K_{t}\|<0.5
by Lemma
3.7
together with the condition on
\tau
that
\tau(1-\sqrt{\mu_{t}/\nu_{t}})^{\tau-2}\leq\gamma_{H}/(4\Upsilon_{H})
, and
\|B_{t}^{-1}\|\leq 1/\gamma_{H}
in (
A.27
). For the first term in (
B.7
), we need the following technical lemma to proceed.

Lemma B.7
.

Let
\Omega_{t}\coloneqq B_{t}^{-1}\mathbb{E}[(g_{t}-\nabla f_{t})(g_{t}-\nabla f_{t})^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]B_{t}^{-1}
. Under the conditions of Theorem
4.3
, we have the following convergence as
t\rightarrow\infty
:

\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})\Omega_{t}(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a.s.}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{K}^{\star}-K^{\star})\Omega^{\star}(\widetilde{K}^{\star}-K^{\star})^{\top}].

By Lemma
B.7
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})B_{t}^{-1}(g_{t}-\nabla f_{t})(g_{t}-\nabla f_{t})^{\top}B_{t}^{-1}(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})B_{t}^{-1}\mathbb{E}[(g_{t}-\nabla f_{t})(g_{t}-\nabla f_{t})^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}\cup\sigma(\{S_{t,j}\}_{j})]B_{t}^{-1}(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})B_{t}^{-1}\mathbb{E}[(g_{t}-\nabla f_{t})(g_{t}-\nabla f_{t})^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]B_{t}^{-1}(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})^{\top})\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a.s.}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{K}^{\star}-K^{\star})\Omega^{\star}(\widetilde{K}^{\star}-K^{\star})^{\top}]=\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{K}^{\star}\Omega^{\star}(\widetilde{K}^{\star})^{\top}]-K^{\star}\Omega^{\star}K^{\star}.

(B.57)

Here,
\widetilde{K}^{\star}
is defined analogously to
\widetilde{K}_{t}
in (
3.2
), but evaluated at
(\alpha^{\star},\beta^{\star},\gamma^{\star})
and
B^{\star}
; see Section
4.1
for more details. As a result, substituting (
B.7
), (
B.7
), (
B.7
) and (
B.7
) into (
B.7
) yields

\mathcal{J}_{2,t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a.s.}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{K}^{\star}\Omega^{\star}(\widetilde{K}^{\star})^{\top}]-K^{\star}\Omega^{\star}K^{\star}.

(B.58)

Combining (
B.52
) and (
B.58
) with (
B.7
), we obtain

\mathbb{E}[\bm{\theta}_{t}\bm{\theta}_{t}^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a.s.}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathbb{E}[(I-\widetilde{K}^{\star})\Omega^{\star}(I-\widetilde{K}^{\star})^{\top}],

(B.59)

where we use the definition
K^{\star}\coloneqq\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{K}^{\star}]
. This completes the proof.

B.8
Proof of Lemma
B.7

By (
B.51
) and Lemma
4.1
, we know
\Omega_{t}\to\Omega^{\star}
almost surely. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})\Omega_{t}(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]-\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{K}^{\star}-K^{\star})\Omega^{\star}(\widetilde{K}^{\star}-K^{\star})^{\top}]\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{K}^{\star}-K^{\star})\Omega_{t}(\widetilde{K}^{\star}-K^{\star})^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]-\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})\Omega^{\star}(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\right\|

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})\Omega^{\star}(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]-\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{K}^{\star}-K^{\star})\Omega^{\star}(\widetilde{K}^{\star}-K^{\star})^{\top}]\right\|.

(B.60)

For the first term on the right-hand side of (
B.8
), we have

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})\Omega_{t}(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]-\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})\Omega^{\star}(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})(\Omega_{t}-\Omega^{\star})(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\|(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})(\Omega_{t}-\Omega^{\star})(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})^{\top}\|\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq(C_{K}+0.5)^{2}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Omega_{t}-\Omega^{\star}\|\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}\right]=(C_{K}+0.5)^{2}\|\Omega_{t}-\Omega^{\star}\|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a.s.}}{{\rightarrow}}0.

(B.61)

Here, the third inequality follows from
\|\widetilde{K}_{t}\|\leq C_{K}
and
\|K_{t}\|<0.5
(under
\tau(1-\sqrt{\mu_{t}/\nu_{t}})^{\tau-2}\leq\gamma_{H}/(4\Upsilon_{H})
). For the second term on the right-hand side of (
B.8
), we have

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})\Omega^{\star}(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]-\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{K}^{\star}-K^{\star})\Omega^{\star}(\widetilde{K}^{\star}-K^{\star})^{\top}]\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})\Omega^{\star}(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]-\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{K}^{\star}-K^{\star})\Omega^{\star}(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\right\|

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{K}^{\star}-K^{\star})\Omega^{\star}(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]-\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{K}^{\star}-K^{\star})\Omega^{\star}(\widetilde{K}^{\star}-K^{\star})^{\top}]\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\Omega^{\star}\|\cdot(C_{K}+0.5)\cdot(\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{K}_{t}-\widetilde{K}^{\star}\|\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]+\|K_{t}-K^{\star}\|)

\displaystyle\quad+\|\Omega^{\star}\|\cdot(C_{K}+0.5)\cdot(\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{K}_{t}-\widetilde{K}^{\star}\|\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]+\|K_{t}-K^{\star}\|).

By Lemma
4.1
, we know
K_{t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a.s.}}{{\rightarrow}}K^{\star}
as
t\rightarrow\infty
. Also, in the same spirit as the proof of Lemma
4.1
(see also (
B.15
)), we obtain
\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{K}_{t}-\widetilde{K}^{\star}\|\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\lesssim\|B_{t}-B^{\star}\|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a.s.}}{{\rightarrow}}0
. Thus, the above display leads to

\left\|\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})\Omega^{\star}(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]-\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{K}^{\star}-K^{\star})\Omega^{\star}(\widetilde{K}^{\star}-K^{\star})^{\top}]\right\|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a.s.}}{{\rightarrow}}0.

(B.62)

Substituting (
B.8
) and (
B.62
) into (
B.8
) yields

\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})\Omega_{t}(\widetilde{K}_{t}-K_{t})^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a.s.}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{K}^{\star}-K^{\star})\Omega^{\star}(\widetilde{K}^{\star}-K^{\star})^{\top}]\quad\text{as }t\rightarrow\infty.

This completes the proof.

B.9
Proof of Proposition
4.4

\bullet

Proof of (a).
We plug
\widetilde{K}^{\star}=K^{\star}=\bm{0}
into (
4.2
) and have
[(1-\zeta)I]\Sigma^{\star}+\Sigma^{\star}[(1-\zeta)I]=\Omega^{\star}
. Thus, for
\varphi\in(0.5,1)
,
\zeta=0
, then
\Sigma^{\star}=0.5\Omega^{\star}
. For
\varphi=1
,
\zeta=1/(2C_{\varphi})
, then
\Sigma^{\star}=\frac{C_{\varphi}\Omega^{\star}}{2C_{\varphi}-1}
. Furthermore, when
\varphi=C_{\varphi}=1
, we have
\Sigma^{\star}=\Omega^{\star}
. Then, we apply the result into Theorem
4.3
, and get that

\sqrt{t}\cdot(\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star})\xrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},\Omega^{\star}).

We complete the proof of (a).

\bullet

Proof of (b).
When
\gamma^{\star}=1
, we follow the similar steps in the proof of Lemma
3.5
, and get that
\widetilde{K}^{\star}=\prod_{j=0}^{\tau-1}(I-B^{\star}S_{j}(S_{j}^{\top}(B^{\star})^{2}S_{j})^{\dagger}S_{j}^{\top}B^{\star})=\tilde{\mathcal{C}}^{\star}
, and
K^{\star}\coloneqq\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{K}^{\star}]=\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\mathcal{C}}^{\star}]
. We apply these results to (
4.2
), and finally get

\left[(I-\mathcal{C}^{\star})-\zeta I\right]\Sigma^{\star}+\Sigma^{\star}\left[(I-\mathcal{C}^{\star})-\zeta I\right]=\mathcal{G}^{\star}.

We complete the proof of (b).

B.10
Proof of Lemma
4.5

The proof is composed of two parts.

\bullet

Part 1: Bound of
\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{4}]
.
For (
A.25
), taking square on both sides and then the conditional expectation given
\mathcal{F}_{t-1}
yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle[(f_{t+1}-f^{\star})^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\leq(f_{t}-f^{\star})^{2}+2\varphi_{t}\mathbb{E}[(f_{t}-f^{\star})\nabla f_{t}^{\top}\bm{z}_{t,\tau}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]+\varphi_{t}^{2}\Upsilon_{H}\mathbb{E}[(f_{t}-f^{\star})\|\bm{z}_{t,\tau}\|^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]

\displaystyle\quad+\varphi_{t}^{2}\mathbb{E}[(\nabla f_{t}^{\top}\bm{z}_{t,\tau})^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]+\varphi_{t}^{3}\Upsilon_{H}\mathbb{E}[\nabla f_{t}^{\top}\bm{z}_{t,\tau}\|\bm{z}_{t,\tau}\|^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]+\frac{\Upsilon_{H}^{2}}{4}\varphi_{t}^{4}\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{z}_{t,\tau}\|^{4}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}].

(B.63)

We rearrange the terms on the right-hand side by the order of
\varphi_{t}
and then analyze each of them.

\bullet\bullet

Term 1:

2\varphi_{t}\mathbb{E}[(f_{t}-f^{\star})\nabla f_{t}^{\top}\bm{z}_{t,\tau}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]
.
For Term 1, we have

\displaystyle 2\varphi_{t}\mathbb{E}[(f_{t}-f^{\star})\nabla f_{t}^{\top}\bm{z}_{t,\tau}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{equ:45}}}{{\leq}}-\frac{1}{\Upsilon_{H}}\varphi_{t}(f_{t}-f^{\star})\|\nabla f_{t}\|^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{equ:46}}}{{\leq}}-\frac{2\gamma_{H}}{\Upsilon_{H}}\varphi_{t}(f_{t}-f^{\star})^{2}.

(B.64)

\bullet\bullet

Term 2:
\varphi_{t}^{2}\Upsilon_{H}\mathbb{E}[(f_{t}-f^{\star})\|\bm{z}_{t,\tau}\|^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]+\varphi_{t}^{2}\mathbb{E}[(\nabla f_{t}^{\top}\bm{z}_{t,\tau})^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]
. For Term 2, we have
