\displaystyle\varphi_{t}^{2}\Upsilon_{H}\mathbb{E}[(f_{t}-f^{\star})\|\bm{z}_{t,\tau}\|^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]+\varphi_{t}^{2}\mathbb{E}[(\nabla f_{t}^{\top}\bm{z}_{t,\tau})^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\leq\varphi_{t}^{2}\mathbb{E}[(\Upsilon_{H}(f_{t}-f^{\star})+\|\nabla f_{t}\|^{2})\|\bm{z}_{t,\tau}\|^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{equ:59}}}}{{\leq}}\;3\Upsilon_{H}\varphi_{t}^{2}(f_{t}-f^{\star})\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{z}_{t,\tau}\|^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\leq\frac{\gamma_{H}}{\Upsilon_{H}}\varphi_{t}(f_{t}-f^{\star})^{2}+\frac{9\Upsilon_{H}^{3}}{4\gamma_{H}}\varphi_{t}^{3}\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{z}_{t,\tau}\|^{4}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}],

(B.65)

where the last inequality is due to Young’s inequality.

\bullet\bullet

Term 3:
\varphi_{t}^{3}\Upsilon_{H}\mathbb{E}[\nabla f_{t}^{\top}\bm{z}_{t,\tau}\|\bm{z}_{t,\tau}\|^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]
. By Young’s inequality and (
A.24
), we obtain

\displaystyle\varphi_{t}^{3}\Upsilon_{H}\mathbb{E}[\nabla f_{t}^{\top}\bm{z}_{t,\tau}\|\bm{z}_{t,\tau}\|^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\leq\varphi_{t}^{3}\Upsilon_{H}\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla f_{t}\|\|\bm{z}_{t,\tau}\|^{3}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varphi_{t}^{3}\Upsilon_{H}}{2}\left(\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla f_{t}\|^{2}\|\bm{z}_{t,\tau}\|^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]+\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{z}_{t,\tau}\|^{4}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varphi_{t}^{3}\Upsilon_{H}}{2}\left(\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla f_{t}\|^{4}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{z}_{t,\tau}\|^{4}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]+\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{z}_{t,\tau}\|^{4}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\varphi_{t}^{3}\Upsilon_{H}}{4}\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla f_{t}\|^{4}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]+\frac{3\varphi_{t}^{3}\Upsilon_{H}}{4}\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{z}_{t,\tau}\|^{4}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{equ:59}}}}{{\leq}}\;\varphi_{t}^{3}\Upsilon_{H}^{3}(f_{t}-f^{\star})^{2}+\frac{3\Upsilon_{H}}{4}\varphi_{t}^{3}\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{z}_{t,\tau}\|^{4}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}].

(B.66)

\bullet\bullet

Term 4:

\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{z}_{t,\tau}\|^{4}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]
.
Following the analysis in (
A.5
), we apply Assumption
3.2
with
q_{g}=4
and obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{z}_{t,\tau}\|^{4}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(1+C_{K})^{4}}{\gamma_{H}^{4}}\mathbb{E}[\|g_{t}\|^{4}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\leq\frac{8(1+C_{K})^{4}}{\gamma_{H}^{4}}(\|\nabla f_{t}\|^{4}+\mathbb{E}[\|g_{t}-\nabla f_{t}\|^{4}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}])

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{equ:59}}}}{{\leq}}\;\frac{8(1+C_{K})^{4}}{\gamma_{H}^{4}}(4\Upsilon_{H}^{2}(f_{t}-f^{\star})^{2}+C_{g,1}\|\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{4}+C_{g,2})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{equ:46}}}}{{\leq}}\;\frac{8(1+C_{K})^{4}}{\gamma_{H}^{4}}(4\Upsilon_{H}^{2}(f_{t}-f^{\star})^{2}+\frac{4C_{g,1}}{\gamma^{2}_{H}}(f_{t}-f^{\star})^{2}+C_{g,2})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{32(1+C_{K})^{4}\left[\Upsilon_{H}^{2}+C_{g,1}/\gamma_{H}^{2}\right]}{\gamma_{H}^{4}}(f_{t}-f^{\star})^{2}+\frac{8(1+C_{K})^{4}C_{g,2}}{\gamma_{H}^{4}}

\displaystyle\eqqcolon C_{1}(f_{t}-f^{\star})^{2}+C_{2},

(B.67)

where

C_{1}\coloneqq\frac{32(1+C_{K})^{4}\left[\Upsilon_{H}^{2}+C_{g,1}/\gamma_{H}^{2}\right]}{\gamma_{H}^{4}},\quad\quad C_{2}\coloneqq\frac{8(1+C_{K})^{4}C_{g,2}}{\gamma_{H}^{4}}.

Substituting (
B.64
), (
B.10
), (
B.10
) and (
B.10
) into (
B.10
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(f_{t+1}-f^{\star})^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]

\displaystyle\leq(f_{t}-f^{\star})^{2}-\left(\frac{\gamma_{H}}{\Upsilon_{H}}\varphi_{t}-\frac{9\Upsilon_{H}^{3}C_{1}+4\Upsilon_{H}^{3}\gamma_{H}+3C_{1}\Upsilon_{H}\gamma_{H}}{4\gamma_{H}}\varphi_{t}^{3}-\frac{\Upsilon_{H}^{2}C_{1}}{4}\varphi_{t}^{4}\right)(f_{t}-f^{\star})^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{9\Upsilon_{H}^{3}C_{2}+3C_{2}\Upsilon_{H}\gamma_{H}}{4\gamma_{H}}\varphi_{t}^{3}+\frac{\Upsilon_{H}^{2}C_{2}}{4}\varphi_{t}^{4}.

(B.68)

Since
\varphi_{t}=C_{\varphi}/(t+1)^{\varphi}
for
\varphi\in(0.5,1)
, for
t
large enough we have

\frac{9\Upsilon_{H}^{3}C_{1}+4\Upsilon_{H}^{3}\gamma_{H}+3C_{1}\Upsilon_{H}\gamma_{H}}{4\gamma_{H}}\varphi_{t}^{2}+\frac{\Upsilon_{H}^{2}C_{1}}{4}\varphi_{t}^{3}<\frac{\gamma_{H}}{2\Upsilon_{H}}.

(B.69)

Substituting (
B.69
) into (
B.10
) and then taking the expectation on both sides gives us

\mathbb{E}\left[(f_{t+1}-f^{\star})^{2}\right]\leq\left(1-\frac{\gamma_{H}}{2\Upsilon_{H}}\varphi_{t}\right)\mathbb{E}[(f_{t}-f^{\star})^{2}]+\frac{9\Upsilon_{H}^{3}C_{2}+3\gamma_{H}\Upsilon_{H}C_{2}+\gamma_{H}\Upsilon_{H}^{2}C_{2}C_{\varphi}}{4\gamma_{H}}\varphi_{t}^{3}.

(B.70)

Again by
34
, we obtain

\limsup_{t\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\mathbb{E}[(f_{t}-f^{\star})^{2}]}{\varphi_{t}^{2}}\lesssim 1\implies\mathbb{E}[(f_{t}-f^{\star})^{2}]\lesssim\varphi_{t}^{2}.

By (
A.23
), we further have

\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{4}\right]\lesssim\mathbb{E}[(f_{t}-f^{\star})^{2}]\lesssim\varphi_{t}^{2}.

(B.71)

\bullet

Part 2: Bound of
\mathbb{E}[\|B_{t}-B^{\star}\|^{4}]
.
By the construction of
B_{t}
in (
2.1
), we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\|B_{t}-B^{\star}\|^{4}\right]\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}(H_{i}-\nabla^{2}F_{i})\right\|^{4}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}(\nabla^{2}F_{i}-\nabla^{2}F^{\star})\right\|^{4}\right].

(B.72)

\bullet\bullet

Term 1:

\mathbb{E}[\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}(H_{i}-\nabla^{2}F_{i})\|^{4}]
.
For Term 1, note that
H_{i}-\nabla^{2}F_{i}
is a martingale difference sequence and Assumption
3.3
with
q_{H}=4
implies

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|H_{i}-\nabla^{2}F_{i}\right\|_{F}^{4}\right]\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|H_{i}-\nabla^{2}F_{i}\right\|^{4}\right]\leq C_{H,1}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{x}_{i}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{4}\right]+C_{H,2}.

Therefore, same as in
9
, we apply
48
and obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}(H_{i}-\nabla^{2}F_{i})\right\|^{4}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}(H_{i}-\nabla^{2}F_{i})\right\|_{F}^{4}\right]\lesssim\frac{1}{t^{4}}\left[\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|H_{i}-\nabla^{2}F_{i}\right\|_{F}^{4}\right]\right)^{1/2}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{t^{4}}\left(\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}C_{H,1}^{1/2}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{x}_{i}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{4}\right]\right)^{1/2}\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{t^{4}}\left(\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}C_{H,2}^{1/2}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{equ:cov_lemma_p1}}}}{{\lesssim}}\frac{1}{t^{4}}\left(\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\varphi_{i}\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{t^{2}}\lesssim\frac{1}{t^{4}}\cdot t^{2(1-\varphi)}+\frac{1}{t^{2}}\lesssim\frac{1}{t^{2}},

(B.73)

where the second last inequality follows from the fact that
\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\varphi_{i}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}1/i^{\varphi}di\lesssim t^{1-\varphi}
.

\bullet\bullet

Term 2:

\mathbb{E}[\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}(\nabla^{2}F_{i}-\nabla^{2}F^{\star})\|^{4}]
. For Term 2, by the
\Upsilon_{L}
-Lipschitz continuity of
\nabla^{2}F(\bm{x})
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}(\nabla^{2}F_{i}-\nabla^{2}F^{\star})\right\|^{4}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\left\|\nabla^{2}F_{i}-\nabla^{2}F^{\star}\right\|\right)^{4}\right]\leq\frac{\Upsilon_{L}^{4}}{t^{4}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\|\bm{x}_{i}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|\right)^{4}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\Upsilon_{L}^{4}}{t^{4}}\left(\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{x}_{i}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{4}\right]\right)^{1/4}\right)^{4}\quad(\text{by H\"{o}lder's inequality})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{equ:cov_lemma_p1}}}}{{\lesssim}}\;\;\frac{1}{t^{4}}\left(\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\sqrt{\varphi_{i}}\right)^{4}\lesssim\frac{1}{t^{4}}t^{4(1-\varphi/2)}\lesssim\varphi_{t}^{2}.

(B.74)

Plugging (
B.10
) and (
B.10
) into (
B.72
) gives us

\mathbb{E}\left[\|B_{t}-B^{\star}\|^{4}\right]\lesssim\frac{1}{t^{2}}+\varphi_{t}^{2}\lesssim\varphi_{t}^{2}.

(B.75)

This, together with (
B.71
), completes the proof for both parts.

B.11
Proof of Theorem
4.6

Without loss of generality, we suppose
\varphi_{t}\leq 1
,
\forall\,t\geq 0
. The weighted sample covariance matrix
\widehat{\Sigma}_{t}
can be decomposed as

\widehat{\Sigma}_{t}=\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i-1}}(\bm{x}_{i}-\bm{x}^{\star})(\bm{x}_{i}-\bm{x}^{\star})^{\top}+\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i-1}}(\bar{\bm{x}}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star})(\bar{\bm{x}}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star})^{\top}\\
-\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i-1}}(\bm{x}_{i}-\bm{x}^{\star})(\bar{\bm{x}}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star})^{\top}-\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i-1}}(\bar{\bm{x}}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star})(\bm{x}_{i}-\bm{x}^{\star})^{\top}.

(B.76)

By (
B.6
), we have

\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varphi_{t-1}}}(\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star})=\mathcal{L}_{1,t}+\mathcal{L}_{2,t},

where

\mathcal{L}_{1,t}=\mathcal{I}_{2,t}+\mathcal{I}_{4,t}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varphi_{t-1}}}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\varphi_{i}\Pi_{t,i+1}\bm{\theta}_{i},\quad\quad\mathcal{L}_{2,t}=\mathcal{I}_{1,t}+\mathcal{I}_{3,t}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varphi_{t-1}}}\left(\Pi_{t,0}\Delta_{0}+\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\varphi_{i}\Pi_{t,i+1}\bm{\delta}_{i}\right).

(B.77)

Therefore, we can further decompose the first term on the right-hand side of (
B.76
) as follows:

\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i-1}}(\bm{x}_{i}-\bm{x}^{\star})(\bm{x}_{i}-\bm{x}^{\star})^{\top}=\sum_{k=1}^{2}\sum_{l=1}^{2}\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathcal{L}_{k,i}\mathcal{L}_{l,i}^{\top}.

(B.78)

The following lemma shows that the dominant term
\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathcal{L}_{1,i}\mathcal{L}_{1,i}^{\top}
converges to the limiting covariance matrix
\Sigma^{\star}
and establishes the convergence rate.

Lemma B.8
.

Under the conditions of Theorem
4.6
, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathcal{L}_{1,i}\mathcal{L}_{1,i}^{\top}-\Sigma^{\star}\right\|\right]\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{t\varphi_{t}}}.

With Lemma
B.8
, the next two lemmas show that
\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i-1}}(\bm{x}_{i}-\bm{x}^{\star})(\bm{x}_{i}-\bm{x}^{\star})^{T}
converges to
\Sigma^{\star}
, and the remaining terms are negligible as
\bar{\bm{x}}_{t}
converges to
\bm{x}^{\star}
fast.

Lemma B.9
.

Under the conditions of Theorem
4.6
, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i-1}}(\bm{x}_{i}-\bm{x}^{\star})(\bm{x}_{i}-\bm{x}^{\star})^{\top}-\Sigma^{\star}\right\|\right]\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{t\varphi_{t}}}

(B.79)

and

\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i-1}}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{x}_{i}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{2}\right]\lesssim 1.

(B.80)

Lemma B.10
.

Under the conditions of Theorem
4.6
, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i-1}}(\bar{\bm{x}}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star})(\bar{\bm{x}}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star})^{\top}\right\|\right]\leq\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i-1}}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bar{\bm{x}}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{2}\right]\lesssim\frac{1}{t\varphi_{t}}.

(B.81)

By the decomposition (
B.76
) of
\widehat{\Sigma}_{t}
, we apply the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality and obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{t}-\Sigma^{\star}\|]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i-1}}(\bm{x}_{i}-\bm{x}^{\star})(\bm{x}_{i}-\bm{x}^{\star})^{\top}-\Sigma^{\star}\right\|\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i-1}}(\bar{\bm{x}}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star})(\bar{\bm{x}}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star})^{\top}\right\|\right]

\displaystyle\quad+2\sqrt{\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i-1}}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{x}_{i}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{2}\right]}\sqrt{\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i-1}}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bar{\bm{x}}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{2}\right]}.

(B.82)

Plugging (
B.79
), (
B.80
), (
B.81
) into the above display, we obtain

\mathbb{E}[\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{t}-\Sigma^{\star}\|]\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{t\varphi_{t}}}.

This completes the proof.

B.12
Proof of Lemma
B.8

We define

\widetilde{C}_{k}^{\star}\coloneqq\prod_{j=0}^{\tau-1}\begin{pmatrix}(1-\alpha^{\star})(I-Z_{k,j}^{\star})&\alpha^{\star}(I-Z_{k,j}^{\star})\\
(1-\alpha^{\star})(1-\beta^{\star})I-(1-\alpha^{\star})\gamma^{\star}Z_{k,j}^{\star}&(\alpha^{\star}+\beta^{\star}-\alpha^{\star}\beta^{\star})I-\alpha^{\star}\gamma^{\star}Z_{k,j}^{\star}\end{pmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{2d\times 2d},

(B.83)

where
Z_{k,j}^{\star}=B^{\star}S_{k,j}(S_{k,j}^{\top}(B^{\star})^{2}S_{k,j})^{\dagger}S_{k,j}^{\top}B^{\star}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
and
S_{k,j}
is the same sketching matrix in
\widetilde{Z}_{k,j}
in (
3.2
). Then, we let

\widetilde{K}_{k}^{\star}\coloneqq\begin{pmatrix}I&\bm{0}\end{pmatrix}\widetilde{C}_{k}^{\star}\begin{pmatrix}I\\
I\end{pmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}.

(B.84)

Due to the independence of
\{S_{k,j}\}_{j}
, it is obvious that
\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{C}_{k}^{\star}]=C^{\star}
and
\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{K}_{k}^{\star}]=K^{\star}
, where
K^{\star}
is defined in Section
4.1
and is constructed from
C^{\star}
in the same manner as above. Then, we can define

\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k}\coloneqq-(I-\widetilde{K}^{\star}_{k})(B^{\star})^{-1}\nabla F(\bm{x}^{\star};\xi_{k})\quad\text{ and }\quad\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k}\coloneqq\bm{\theta}_{k}-\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k}.

(B.85)

Note that
\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k}
and
\bm{\theta}_{k}
share the same randomness but
\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k}
is constructed at
\bm{x}^{\star}
instead of
\bm{x}_{k}
.

In the following, we decompose
\mathcal{L}_{1,i}
as

\mathcal{L}_{1,i}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varphi_{i-1}}}\sum_{k=0}^{i-1}\varphi_{k}\Pi_{i,k+1}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varphi_{i-1}}}\sum_{k=0}^{i-1}\varphi_{k}\Pi_{i,k+1}\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k}\eqqcolon\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{1,i}+\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{1,i}.

(B.86)

Intuitively, as
\bm{x}_{i}\rightarrow\bm{x}^{\star}
,
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{1,i}
should serve as a good approximation to
\mathcal{L}_{1,i}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{1,i}
could be negligible. The next two lemmas provide bounds for
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{1,i}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{1,i}
, respectively.

Lemma B.11
.

Under the conditions of Theorem
4.6
, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{1,i}\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{1,i}^{\top}-\Sigma^{\star}\right\|\right]\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{t\varphi_{t}}}.

Lemma B.12
.

Under the conditions of Theorem
4.6
, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{1,i}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{1,i}^{\top}\right\|\right]\leq\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{1,i}\|^{2}\right]\lesssim\varphi_{t}.

By the decomposition (
B.86
), we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathcal{L}_{1,i}\mathcal{L}_{1,i}^{\top}-\Sigma^{\star}\right\|\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{1,i}\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{1,i}^{\top}-\Sigma^{\star}\right\|\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{1,i}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{1,i}^{\top}\right\|\right]+2\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\ \widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{1,i}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{1,i}^{\top}\right\|\right].

(B.87)

Applying the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality to the last term, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{1,i}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{1,i}^{\top}\right\|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\|\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{1,i}\|\|\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{1,i}\|\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\sqrt{\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\|\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{1,i}\|^{2}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\|\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{1,i}\|^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{1,i}\|^{2}\right]}\sqrt{\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{1,i}\|^{2}\right]}.

(B.88)

Given Lemmas
B.11
and
B.12
, we only need to bound
\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{1,i}\|^{2}]
. We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{1,i}\|^{2}]\;\;

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{appen:A4:equ2}}}}{{=}}\;\;\frac{1}{\varphi_{i-1}}\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}=0}^{i-1}\varphi_{k_{1}}\varphi_{k_{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k_{1}}^{\top}\Pi_{i,k_{1}+1}^{\top}\Pi_{i,k_{2}+1}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k_{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\varphi_{i-1}}\sum_{k=0}^{i-1}\varphi_{k}^{2}\,\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\Pi_{i,k+1}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]\leq\frac{1}{\varphi_{i-1}}\sum_{k=0}^{i-1}\varphi_{k}^{2}\left\|\Pi_{i,k+1}\right\|^{2}\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k}\|^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\varphi_{i-1}}\sum_{k=0}^{i-1}\varphi_{k}^{2}\prod_{l=k+1}^{i-1}(1-\varphi_{l}\lambda_{m})^{2}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k}\|^{4}]},

(B.89)

where the second equality uses the fact that
\{\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k}\}
are mean zero and independent, and the last inequality is the direct application of Jensen’s inequality. Next, we will bound the fourth-order moment of
\|\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k}\|
. We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|\nabla F(\bm{x}^{\star};\xi_{k})\|^{4}\right]\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\|\nabla F(\bm{x}^{\star};\xi_{k})-\nabla F(\bm{x}_{k};\xi_{k})\|^{4}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\|\nabla F(\bm{x}_{k};\xi_{k})-\nabla F_{k}\|^{4}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\|\nabla F_{k}-\nabla F^{\star}\|^{4}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\Upsilon_{H}^{4}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{x}_{k}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{4}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{x}_{k}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{4}\right]+1+\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{x}_{k}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{4}\right]\quad(\text{by Assumptions~\ref{ass:1} and~\ref{ass:2} with $q_{g}=4$})

\displaystyle\lesssim 1+\varphi_{k}^{2}\quad(\text{by Lemma~\ref{sec4:lem1}})

\displaystyle\lesssim 1.

(B.90)

Then, by (
3.2
) and (
A.5
), we have

\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k}\|^{4}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{appen:A4:equ3}}}{{\leq}}\mathbb{E}\left[\|I-\widetilde{K}_{k}^{\star}\|^{4}\|(B^{\star})^{-1}\|^{4}\|\nabla F(\bm{x}^{\star};\xi_{k})\|^{4}\right]\leq\frac{(1+C_{K})^{4}}{\gamma_{H}^{4}}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\nabla F(\bm{x}^{\star};\xi_{k})\|^{4}\right]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{appen:A4:equ22}}}{{\lesssim}}1.

(B.91)

Plugging (
B.91
) into (
B.12
) gives us

\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{1,i}\|^{2}]\lesssim\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i}}\sum_{k=0}^{i}\varphi_{k}^{2}\prod_{l=k+1}^{i}(1-\varphi_{l}\lambda_{m})^{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i}}\cdot\varphi_{i}=1,

(B.92)

where the second inequality is the direct application of
41
. Combining (
B.12
), (
B.92
) and Lemma
B.12
, we obtain

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i}}\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{1,i}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{1,i}^{\top}\right\|\right]\lesssim\sqrt{\varphi_{t}}.

(B.93)

Combining the above display with Lemmas
B.11
,
B.12
, and (
B.87
), we obtain

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathcal{L}_{1,i}\mathcal{L}_{1,i}^{\top}-\Sigma^{\star}\right\|\right]\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{t\varphi_{t}}}.

(B.94)

This completes the proof.

B.13
Proof of Lemma
B.11

Consider the eigenvalue decomposition of
\mathcal{R}=I-K^{\star}=U\Lambda U^{\top}
with
\Lambda=\text{diag}(\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{d})
. We have

\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{1,i}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varphi_{i-1}}}\sum_{k=0}^{i-1}\varphi_{k}\prod_{l=k+1}^{i-1}(I-\varphi_{l}\mathcal{R})\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varphi_{i-1}}}U\sum_{k=0}^{i-1}\varphi_{k}\prod_{l=k+1}^{i-1}(I-\varphi_{l}\Lambda)U^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k}.

(B.95)

Let
\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}=U^{\top}\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{1,i}
and
\Xi^{\star}=U^{\top}\Gamma^{\star}U
with
\Gamma^{\star}=\mathbb{E}[(I-\widetilde{K}^{\star})\Omega^{\star}(I-\widetilde{K}^{\star})^{\top}]
defined in (
4.1
). It is known by
41
that the limiting covariance
\Sigma^{\star}
in (
4.2
) can be explicitly expressed as

\Sigma^{\star}=U\left(\Theta\circ\Xi^{\star}\right)U^{\top}\quad\text{ with }\quad\Theta_{k,l}=\frac{1}{\lambda_{k}+\lambda_{l}-\mathbf{1}_{\{\varphi=1\}}/C_{\varphi}},

where
\circ
denotes the matrix Hadamard product. Therefore, we obtain

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{1,i}\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{1,i}^{\top}-\Sigma^{\star}\right\|\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}^{\top}-\Theta\circ\Xi^{\star}\right\|\right]\\
\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}^{\top}-\Theta\circ\Xi^{\star}\right\|_{F}\right]\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}^{\top}-\Theta\circ\Xi^{\star}\right\|_{F}^{2}\right]}.

(B.96)

For the term above, we perform bias-variance decomposition as follows:

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}^{\top}-\Theta\circ\Xi^{\star}\right\|_{F}^{2}\right]=\sum_{p,q=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{i,p}\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{i,q}-\Theta_{p,q}\Xi^{\star}_{p,q}\right)^{2}\right]=I+II,

(B.97)

where

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{p,q=1}^{d}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{i,p}\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{i,q}\right)^{2}\right]-\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{i,p}\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{i,q}\right]\right)^{2}\right\}\quad(\text{variance}),

(B.98a)

\displaystyle II

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{p,q=1}^{d}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{i,p}\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{i,q}\right]-\Theta_{p,q}\Xi^{\star}_{p,q}\right)^{2}\quad(\text{bias}^{2}),

(B.98b)

where
\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{i,p}
represents the
p
-th element in
\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}
.

We start with the analysis for the Term
II
. By (
B.95
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{i,p}\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{i,q}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i-1}}\sum_{k_{1}=0}^{i-1}\sum_{k_{2}=0}^{i-1}\prod_{l_{1}=k_{1}+1}^{i-1}(1-\varphi_{l_{1}}\lambda_{p})\prod_{l_{2}=k_{2}+1}^{i-1}(1-\varphi_{l_{2}}\lambda_{q})\varphi_{k_{1}}\varphi_{k_{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(U^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k_{1}}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k_{2}}^{\top}U\right)_{p,q}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i-1}}\sum_{k=0}^{i-1}\prod_{l=k+1}^{i-1}(1-\varphi_{l}\lambda_{p})(1-\varphi_{l}\lambda_{q})\varphi_{k}^{2}\Xi^{\star}_{p,q},

(B.99)

where the second equality is due to the definition of
\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k}
in (
B.85
) and the independence between the random sample
\xi_{k}
and the sketching matrices
\{S_{k,j}\}_{j}
. In particular, we have

\mathbb{E}[U^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k_{1}}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k_{2}}^{\top}U]=0\;\text{ for }\;k_{1}\neq k_{2}\quad\quad\text{ and }\quad\quad\mathbb{E}[U^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k}^{\top}U]=\Xi^{\star}\;\text{ for }\;k_{1}=k_{2}=k.

We give the following technical lemma which will be used for upper bounding the Term
II
.

Lemma B.13

(
30
, Lemma B.3)
.

Suppose
\{\phi_{i}\}_{i}
and
\{\sigma_{i}\}_{i}
are two positive sequences, and
\{\phi_{i}\}_{i}
satisfies
\lim_{i\rightarrow\infty}i(1-\phi_{i-1}/\phi_{i})=\phi<0
for a constant
\phi
. Let
\varphi_{i}=c_{\varphi}/(i+1)^{\varphi}+o(1/(i+1)^{\varphi})
for constants
c_{\varphi}>0
and
\varphi\in(0,1)
. For any
l\geq 1
, we have

\left|\frac{1}{\phi_{t}}\sum_{i=0}^{t}\prod_{j=i+1}^{t}\prod_{k=1}^{l}(1-\varphi_{j}\sigma_{k})\varphi_{i}\phi_{i}-\frac{1}{\sum_{k=1}^{l}\sigma_{k}}\right|\lesssim\begin{cases}\varphi_{t},&\varphi\in(0,0.5),\\
\left(0.5-\frac{\phi/c_{\varphi}^{2}}{(\sum_{k=1}^{l}\sigma_{k})^{2}}\right)\varphi_{t},&\varphi=0.5,\\
-\frac{\phi}{(\sum_{k=1}^{l}\sigma_{k})^{2}}\cdot\frac{1}{t\varphi_{t}},&\varphi\in(0.5,1).\end{cases}

With this lemma, we plug (
B.13
) into the definition of term
II
in (
B.98b
), and have

\displaystyle II\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{p,q=1}^{d}\left(\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\left|\frac{1}{\varphi_{i-1}}\sum_{k=0}^{i-1}\prod_{l=k+1}^{i-1}\left(1-\varphi_{l}\lambda_{p}\right)\left(1-\varphi_{l}\lambda_{q}\right)\varphi_{k}^{2}-\Theta_{p,q}\right|\right)^{2}\left(\Xi_{p,q}^{\star}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{p,q=1}^{d}\left(\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\left|\frac{1}{\varphi_{i}}\sum_{k=0}^{i}\prod_{l=k+1}^{i}\left(1-\varphi_{l}\lambda_{p}\right)\left(1-\varphi_{l}\lambda_{q}\right)\varphi_{k}^{2}-\Theta_{p,q}\right|\right)^{2}\left(\Xi_{p,q}^{\star}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{p,q=1}^{d}\left(\frac{\varphi}{(\lambda_{p}+\lambda_{q})^{2}}\cdot\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\frac{1}{i\varphi_{i}}\right)^{2}\left(\Xi_{p,q}^{\star}\right)^{2}\quad(\text{by Lemma~\ref{aux:lem3}})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\frac{1}{i\varphi_{i}}\right)^{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{t^{2}\varphi_{t}^{2}},

(B.100)

where the third inequality also uses the fact that
\lim_{i\rightarrow\infty}i(1-\varphi_{i-1}/\varphi_{i})=-\varphi
, which can be easily verified; and the last inequality is due to the fact that
\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}1/(i\varphi_{i})\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}1/i^{1-\varphi}di\lesssim 1/\varphi_{t}
.

Now, we deal with the Term
I
in (
B.98a
). By (
B.95
), we expand
I
as

\displaystyle I=

\displaystyle\sum_{p,q=1}^{d}\frac{1}{t^{2}}\sum_{i_{1},i_{2}=1}^{t}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i_{1}-1}}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i_{2}-1}}\sum_{k_{1},k_{1}^{\prime}=0}^{i_{1}-1}\sum_{k_{2},k_{2}^{\prime}=0}^{i_{2}-1}\prod_{l_{1}=k_{1}+1}^{i_{1}-1}(1-\varphi_{l_{1}}\lambda_{p})\prod_{l_{1}^{\prime}=k_{1}^{\prime}+1}^{i_{1}-1}(1-\varphi_{l_{1}^{\prime}}\lambda_{q})\prod_{l_{2}=k_{2}+1}^{i_{2}-1}(1-\varphi_{l_{2}}\lambda_{p})\prod_{l_{2}^{\prime}=k_{2}^{\prime}+1}^{i_{2}-1}(1-\varphi_{l_{2}^{\prime}}\lambda_{q})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\varphi_{k_{1}}\varphi_{k_{1}^{\prime}}\varphi_{k_{2}}\varphi_{k_{2}^{\prime}}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[\left(U^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k_{1}}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k_{1}^{\prime}}^{\top}U\right)_{p,q}\left(U^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k_{2}}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k_{2}^{\prime}}^{\top}U\right)_{p,q}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\left(U^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k_{1}}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k_{1}^{\prime}}^{\top}U\right)_{p,q}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\left(U^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k_{2}}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k_{2}^{\prime}}^{\top}U\right)_{p,q}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{p,q=1}^{d}\frac{1}{t^{2}}\sum_{i_{1},i_{2}=0}^{t-1}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i_{1}}}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i_{2}}}\sum_{k_{1},k_{1}^{\prime}=0}^{i_{1}}\sum_{k_{2},k_{2}^{\prime}=0}^{i_{2}}\prod_{l_{1}=k_{1}+1}^{i_{1}}(1-\varphi_{l_{1}}\lambda_{p})\prod_{l_{1}^{\prime}=k_{1}^{\prime}+1}^{i_{1}}(1-\varphi_{l_{1}^{\prime}}\lambda_{q})\prod_{l_{2}=k_{2}+1}^{i_{2}}(1-\varphi_{l_{2}}\lambda_{p})\prod_{l_{2}^{\prime}=k_{2}^{\prime}+1}^{i_{2}}(1-\varphi_{l_{2}^{\prime}}\lambda_{q})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\varphi_{k_{1}}\varphi_{k_{1}^{\prime}}\varphi_{k_{2}}\varphi_{k_{2}^{\prime}}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[\left(U^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k_{1}}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k_{1}^{\prime}}^{\top}U\right)_{p,q}\left(U^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k_{2}}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k_{2}^{\prime}}^{\top}U\right)_{p,q}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\left(U^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k_{1}}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k_{1}^{\prime}}^{\top}U\right)_{p,q}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\left(U^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k_{2}}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k_{2}^{\prime}}^{\top}U\right)_{p,q}\right]\right\}.

(B.101)

Since
\{\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k}\}_{k}
are independent across
k
and
\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k}]=\mathbf{0}
, any configuration for which
\{k_{1},k_{1}^{\prime},k_{2},k_{2}^{\prime}\}
contains a singleton index (appearing only once) yields zero, and disjoint pairs factorize to zero. Therefore, the term in braces can be nonzero only when there are no singletons and the two pairs are not disjoint. In particular, we will consider the following three cases: (1)
k_{1}=k_{1}^{\prime}=k_{2}=k_{2}^{\prime}
; (2)
k_{1}=k_{2},\,k_{1}^{\prime}=k_{2}^{\prime},\,k_{1}\neq k_{1}^{\prime}
; (3)
k_{1}=k_{2}^{\prime},\,k_{1}^{\prime}=k_{2},\,k_{1}\neq k_{1}^{\prime}
.

\bullet

Case 1:

k_{1}=k_{1}^{\prime}=k_{2}=k_{2}^{\prime}=k
.
In this case, since
k_{1}=k_{1}^{\prime}
, we have

\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left(U^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k}^{\top}U\right)_{p,q}\right]\right)^{2}\geq 0\quad\quad\text{ and }\quad\quad\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{p,q=1}^{d}\left(U^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k}^{\top}U\right)_{p,q}^{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k}\|^{4}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{appen:A4:equ5}}}{{\lesssim}}1.

Summing over the indices in
Case 1
and applying the above display, we obtain

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{p,q=1}^{d}\frac{1}{t^{2}}\sum_{i_{1}=0}^{t-1}\sum_{i_{2}=0}^{t-1}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i_{1}}}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i_{2}}}\sum_{k=0}^{i_{1}\wedge i_{2}}\prod_{l_{1}=k+1}^{i_{1}}(1-\varphi_{l_{1}}\lambda_{p})(1-\varphi_{l_{1}}\lambda_{q})\prod_{l_{2}=k+1}^{i_{2}}(1-\varphi_{l_{2}}\lambda_{p})(1-\varphi_{l_{2}}\lambda_{q})\varphi_{k}^{4}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(U^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k}^{\top}U\right)_{p,q}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{t^{2}}\sum_{i_{1}=0}^{t-1}\sum_{i_{2}=0}^{t-1}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i_{1}}\varphi_{i_{2}}}\sum_{k=0}^{i_{1}\wedge i_{2}}\prod_{l_{1}=k+1}^{i_{1}}(1-\varphi_{l_{1}}\lambda_{m})^{2}\prod_{l_{2}=k+1}^{i_{2}}(1-\varphi_{l_{2}}\lambda_{m})^{2}\varphi_{k}^{4}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{t^{2}}\sum_{i_{1}=0}^{t-1}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i_{1}}}\sum_{i_{2}=0}^{i_{1}}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i_{2}}}\prod_{l=i_{2}+1}^{i_{1}}(1-\varphi_{l}\lambda_{m})^{2}\sum_{k=0}^{i_{2}}\prod_{l=k+1}^{i_{2}}(1-\varphi_{l}\lambda_{m})^{4}\varphi_{k}^{4}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{t^{2}}\sum_{i_{1}=0}^{t-1}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i_{1}}}\sum_{i_{2}=0}^{i_{1}}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i_{2}}}\prod_{l=i_{2}+1}^{i_{1}}(1-\varphi_{l}\lambda_{m})^{2}\varphi_{i_{2}}^{3}\lesssim\frac{1}{t^{2}}\sum_{i_{1}=0}^{t-1}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i_{1}}}\varphi_{i_{1}}=\frac{1}{t},

(B.102)

where the two inequalities in the last line are both direct applications of
41
.

\bullet

Case 2:

k_{1}=k_{2},\,k_{1}^{\prime}=k_{2}^{\prime},\,k_{1}\neq k_{1}^{\prime}
.
In this case, since
k_{1}\neq k_{1}^{\prime}
, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(U^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k_{1}}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k_{1}^{\prime}}^{\top}U\right)_{p,q}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\left(U^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k_{2}}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k_{2}^{\prime}}^{\top}U\right)_{p,q}\right]=0\;\text{ and }\;\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{p,q=1}^{d}\left(U^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k_{1}}\right)_{p}^{2}\left(U^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k_{1}^{\prime}}\right)_{q}^{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k_{1}}\|^{2}]\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k_{1}^{\prime}}\|^{2}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{appen:A4:equ5}}}{{\lesssim}}1.

Summing over the indices in
Case 2
and applying the above display yield

\displaystyle I_{2}=\sum_{p,q=1}^{d}\frac{1}{t^{2}}\sum_{i_{1}=0}^{t-1}\sum_{i_{2}=0}^{t-1}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i_{1}}}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i_{2}}}\sum_{k_{1}=0}^{i_{1}\wedge i_{2}}\prod_{l_{1}=k_{1}+1}^{i_{1}}(1-\varphi_{l_{1}}\lambda_{p})\prod_{l_{2}=k_{1}+1}^{i_{2}}(1-\varphi_{l_{2}}\lambda_{p})\varphi_{k_{1}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\times\sum_{k_{1}^{\prime}=0,k_{1}\neq k_{1}^{\prime}}^{i_{1}\wedge i_{2}}\prod_{l_{1}^{\prime}=k_{1}^{\prime}+1}^{i_{1}}(1-\varphi_{l_{1}^{\prime}}\lambda_{q})\prod_{l_{2}^{\prime}=k_{1}^{\prime}+1}^{i_{2}}(1-\varphi_{l_{1}^{\prime}}\lambda_{q})\varphi_{k_{1}^{\prime}}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(U^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k_{1}}\right)_{p}^{2}\left(U^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k_{1}^{\prime}}\right)_{q}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{t^{2}}\sum_{i_{1},i_{2}=0}^{t-1}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i_{1}}\varphi_{i_{2}}}\sum_{k_{1}=0}^{i_{1}\wedge i_{2}}\prod_{l_{1}=k_{1}+1}^{i_{1}}(1-\varphi_{l_{1}}\lambda_{m})\prod_{l_{2}=k_{1}+1}^{i_{2}}(1-\varphi_{l_{2}}\lambda_{m})\varphi_{k_{1}}^{2}\sum_{k_{1}^{\prime}=0,k_{1}\neq k_{1}^{\prime}}^{i_{1}\wedge i_{2}}\prod_{l_{1}^{\prime}=k_{1}^{\prime}+1}^{i_{1}}(1-\varphi_{l_{1}^{\prime}}\lambda_{m})\prod_{l_{2}^{\prime}=k_{1}^{\prime}+1}^{i_{2}}(1-\varphi_{l_{2}^{\prime}}\lambda_{m})\varphi_{k_{1}^{\prime}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{t^{2}}\sum_{i_{1}=0}^{t-1}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i_{1}}}\sum_{i_{2}=0}^{i_{1}}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i_{2}}}\prod_{l=i_{2}+1}^{i_{1}}(1-\varphi_{l}\lambda_{m})^{2}\left\{\sum_{k=0}^{i_{2}}\prod_{l=k+1}^{i_{2}}(1-\varphi_{l}\lambda_{m})^{2}\varphi_{k}^{2}\right\}^{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{t^{2}}\sum_{i_{1}=0}^{t-1}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i_{1}}}\sum_{i_{2}=0}^{i_{1}}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i_{2}}}\prod_{l=i_{2}+1}^{i_{1}}(1-\varphi_{l}\lambda_{m})^{2}\varphi_{i_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{t^{2}}\sum_{i_{1}=0}^{t-1}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i_{1}}}\lesssim\frac{1}{t^{2}}\cdot t^{1+\varphi}\lesssim\frac{1}{t\varphi_{t}},

(B.103)

where the fourth and fifth inequalities are both due to
41
.

\bullet

Case 3:

k_{1}=k_{2}^{\prime},\,k_{1}^{\prime}=k_{2},\,k_{1}\neq k_{1}^{\prime}
.
In this case, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(U^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k_{1}}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k_{1}^{\prime}}^{\top}U\right)_{p,q}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\left(U^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k_{2}}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k_{2}^{\prime}}^{\top}U\right)_{p,q}\right]=0\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{p,q=1}^{d}\left(U^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k_{1}}\right)_{p}^{2}\left(U^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k_{1}^{\prime}}\right)_{q}^{2}\right]=\|\Xi^{\star}\|_{F}^{2}=\|\Gamma^{\star}\|_{F}^{2}\lesssim 1.

Therefore, the analysis of
I_{3}
is identical to that of
I_{2}
and we can conclude that

I_{3}\lesssim\frac{1}{t\varphi_{t}}.

(B.104)

Combining (
B.13
), (
B.13
) and (
B.104
), we obtain

I\lesssim\frac{1}{t}+\frac{1}{t\varphi_{t}}+\frac{1}{t\varphi_{t}}\lesssim\frac{1}{t\varphi_{t}}.

(B.105)

Plugging (
B.13
) and (
B.105
) into (
B.97
), we complete the proof.

B.14
Proof of Lemma
B.12

We present the following lemma to bound
\widehat{\bm{\theta}}^{k}
defined in (
B.85
).

Lemma B.14
.

Under the conditions of Theorem
4.6
, we have

\mathbb{E}[\|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k}\|^{2}]\lesssim\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{x}_{k}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{2}]+\mathbb{E}[\|B_{k}-B^{\star}\|^{2}].

This lemma indicates that the difference between
\bm{\theta}_{k}
and its approximation
\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k}
vanishes. Combining Lemma
B.14
with Lemma
4.5
, we get
\mathbb{E}[\|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k}\|^{2}]\lesssim\varphi_{k}
. Furthermore, we recall the expression of
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{1,i}
in (
B.86
). Since
(\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k})
is a martingale difference sequence, we follow the analysis in (
B.12
) and (
B.92
) and obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{1,i}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{1,i}^{\top}\right\|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathbb{E}[\|\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{1,i}\|^{2}]\leq\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i}}\sum_{k=0}^{i}\varphi_{k}^{2}\prod_{l=k+1}^{i}(1-\varphi_{l}\lambda_{m})^{2}\mathbb{E}[\|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k}\|^{2}]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i}}\sum_{k=0}^{i}\varphi_{k}^{3}\prod_{l=k+1}^{i}(1-\varphi_{l}\lambda_{m})^{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i}}\varphi_{i}^{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{t}t^{1-\varphi}\lesssim\varphi_{t}.

(B.106)

This completes the proof.

B.15
Proof of Lemma
B.14

We expand
\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k}
based on its definition in (
B.85
) as

\displaystyle\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k}

\displaystyle=\bm{\theta}_{k}-\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k}=(I-K_{k})B_{k}^{-1}\nabla f_{k}-(I-K_{k})B_{k}^{-1}\nabla F(\bm{x}_{k};\xi_{k})+(I-\widetilde{K}_{k}^{\star})(B^{\star})^{-1}\nabla F(\bm{x}^{\star};\xi_{k})+\left[\bm{z}_{k,\tau}-(I-K_{k})\Delta\bm{x}_{k}\right]

\displaystyle=(I-K_{k})B_{k}^{-1}\nabla f_{k}-(I-\widetilde{K}_{k})B_{k}^{-1}\left[\nabla F(\bm{x}_{k};\xi_{k})-\nabla F(\bm{x}^{\star};\xi_{k})\right]-\left[(I-\widetilde{K}_{k})B_{k}^{-1}-(I-\widetilde{K}_{k}^{\star})(B^{\star})^{-1}\right]\nabla F(\bm{x}^{\star};\xi_{k}).

Then, we can bound
\|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}^{k}\|^{2}
as

\|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k}\|^{2}\lesssim\|I-K_{k}\|^{2}\|B_{k}^{-1}\|^{2}\|\nabla f_{k}\|^{2}+\|I-\widetilde{K}_{k}\|^{2}\|B_{k}^{-1}\|^{2}\|\nabla F(\bm{x}_{k};\xi_{k})-\nabla F(\bm{x}^{\star};\xi_{k})\|^{2}\\
+\left\|(I-\widetilde{K}_{k})B_{k}^{-1}-(I-\widetilde{K}_{k}^{\star})(B^{\star})^{-1}\right\|^{2}\left\|\nabla F(\bm{x}^{\star};\xi_{k})\right\|^{2}=:I^{\prime}+II^{\prime}+III^{\prime}.

For the first two terms, by Assumption
3.1
, Lemma
3.7
, (
A.24
), (
A.27
), (
A.5
) and the condition on
\tau
, we obtain

\mathbb{E}\left[I^{\prime}\right]\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{x}_{k}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{2}\right]\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathbb{E}\left[II^{\prime}\right]\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{x}_{k}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{2}\right].

(B.107)

Regarding the term
III^{\prime}
, by (
A.27
) and (
A.5
), we have

\displaystyle\left\|(I-\widetilde{K}_{k})B_{k}^{-1}-(I-\widetilde{K}_{k}^{\star})(B^{\star})^{-1}\right\|^{2}\leq\left(\|I-\widetilde{K}_{k}\|\left\|B_{k}^{-1}-(B^{\star})^{-1}\right\|+\left\|(B^{\star})^{-1}\right\|\left\|\widetilde{K}_{k}-\widetilde{K}_{k}^{\star}\right\|\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|I-\widetilde{K}_{k}\|^{2}\left\|B_{k}^{-1}-(B^{\star})^{-1}\right\|^{2}+\left\|(B^{\star})^{-1}\right\|^{2}\left\|\widetilde{K}_{k}-\widetilde{K}_{k}^{\star}\right\|^{2}\quad\text{(by Young’s inequality)}

\displaystyle\leq\|I-\widetilde{K}_{k}\|^{2}\left\|B_{k}^{-1}\right\|^{2}\|(B^{\star})^{-1}\|^{2}\|B_{k}-B^{\star}\|^{2}+\left\|(B^{\star})^{-1}\right\|^{2}\left\|\widetilde{K}_{k}-\widetilde{K}_{k}^{\star}\right\|^{2}\lesssim\|B_{k}-B^{\star}\|^{2}+\|\widetilde{K}_{k}-\widetilde{K}_{k}^{\star}\|^{2}.

Furthermore, applying the tower property gives us

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[III^{\prime}\right]\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\|B_{k}-B^{\star}\|^{2}+\|\widetilde{K}_{k}-\widetilde{K}_{k}^{\star}\|^{2}\right)\|\nabla F(\bm{x}^{\star};\xi_{k})\|^{2}\middle|\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\|B_{k}-B^{\star}\|^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\nabla F(\bm{x}^{\star};\xi_{k})\|^{2}\middle|\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\|\widetilde{K}_{k}-\widetilde{K}_{k}^{\star}\|^{2}\middle|\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\|\nabla F(\bm{x}^{\star};\xi_{k})\|^{2}\middle|\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{appen:A4:equ22}}}}{{\lesssim}}\;\;\mathbb{E}\left[\|B_{k}-B^{\star}\|^{2}\right]+\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{K}_{k}-\widetilde{K}_{k}^{\star}\|^{2}].

(B.108)

Here, the second equality is due to
\|B_{k}-B^{\star}\|
being
\mathcal{F}_{k-1}
-measurable as well as the independence between
\xi_{k}
and
\{S_{k,j}\}_{j=0}^{\tau}
. Similar to the proof of Lemma
4.1
, by the definitions of
\widetilde{C}_{k}
in Section
3.2
and
\widetilde{C}_{k}^{\star}
in (
B.83
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{C}_{k}-\widetilde{C}^{\star}_{k}\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j=0}^{\tau-1}(2+1/\sqrt{\gamma_{S}})^{j}\cdot(|\alpha_{k}-\alpha^{\star}|+|\beta_{k}-\beta^{\star}|+|\gamma_{k}-\gamma^{\star}|+\|Z_{k,j}-\widetilde{Z}^{\star}_{k,j}\|)\cdot(2+1/\sqrt{\gamma_{S}})^{\tau-j-1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j=0}^{\tau-1}(2+1/\sqrt{\gamma_{S}})^{\tau-1}\cdot\left(\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{Z}_{k}-\widetilde{Z}^{\star}\|\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}]+\|Z_{k,j}-\widetilde{Z}^{\star}_{k,j}\|\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j=0}^{\tau-1}\left(\frac{2\|B_{k}-B^{\star}\|}{\gamma_{H}}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\|S\|\|S^{\dagger}\|\right]+\|Z_{k,j}-\widetilde{Z}^{\star}_{k,j}\|\right)\quad(\text{by Lemma \ref{lem:continuity_projection_matrix}})

\displaystyle\lesssim\|B_{k}-B^{\star}\|+\sum_{j=0}^{\tau-1}\|B_{k}S_{k,j}(S_{k,j}^{\top}B_{k}^{2}S_{k,j})^{\dagger}S_{k,j}^{\top}B_{k}-B^{\star}S_{k,j}(S_{k,j}^{\top}(B^{\star})^{2}S_{k,j})^{\dagger}S_{k,j}^{\top}B^{\star}\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|B_{k}-B^{\star}\|+\|B_{k}-B^{\star}\|\sum_{j=0}^{\tau-1}\|S_{k,j}\|\|S_{k,j}^{\dagger}\|\quad(\text{by Lemma \ref{lem:continuity_projection_matrix}}),

(B.109)

where the first inequality also uses the fact that

\prod_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}\;-\;\prod_{i=1}^{n}B_{i}\;=\;\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{k-1}A_{i}\right)\,(A_{k}-B_{k})\,\left(\prod_{j=k+1}^{n}B_{j}\right).

Given the definitions of
\widetilde{K}_{k}
in (
3.2
) and
\widetilde{K}_{k}^{\star}
in (
B.84
), squaring both sides of (
B.15
) and taking conditional expectations give us

\mathbb{E}\left[\|\widetilde{K}_{k}-\widetilde{K}_{k}^{\star}\|^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\begin{pmatrix}I&\bm{0}\end{pmatrix}(\widetilde{C}_{k}-\widetilde{C}_{k}^{\star})\begin{pmatrix}I\\
I\end{pmatrix}\right\|^{2}\ \middle|\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]\\
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{equ:89'}}}}{{\lesssim}}\|B_{k}-B^{\star}\|^{2}+\|B_{k}-B^{\star}\|^{2}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\tau-1}\|S_{k,j}\|\|S_{k,j}^{\dagger}\|\right)^{2}\right]\lesssim\|B_{k}-B^{\star}\|^{2},

(B.110)

where the last inequality is due to Assumption
3.4
with
q_{S}=2
. Plugging (
B.110
) into (
B.15
) yields

\mathbb{E}\left[III^{\prime}\right]\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\|B_{k}-B^{\star}\|^{2}\right].

(B.111)

Combining (
B.107
) and (
B.111
), we complete the proof.

B.16
Proof of Lemma
B.9

Recalling the decomposition (
B.78
), we have shown the consistency of the dominant term
\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathcal{L}_{1,i}\mathcal{L}_{1,i}^{\top}
in Lemma
B.8
. The next lemma suggests that the terms involving
\{\mathcal{L}_{2,i}\}_{i}
are higher order errors.

Lemma B.15
.

Under the conditions of Theorem
4.6
, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathcal{L}_{2,i}\mathcal{L}_{2,i}^{\top}\right\|\right]\leq\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mathcal{L}_{2,i}\|^{2}\right]\lesssim\varphi_{t}.

With the lemma given above, we are now ready to prove the lemma.

\bullet

Proof of (
B.79
).

By the decomposition (
B.78
), we follow similar procedures given in (
B.87
) and (
B.12
) and have

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i-1}}(\bm{x}_{i}-\bm{x}^{\star})(\bm{x}_{i}-\bm{x}^{\star})^{\top}-\Sigma^{\star}\right\|\right]\\
\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathcal{L}_{1,i}\mathcal{L}_{1,i}^{\top}-\Sigma^{\star}\right\|\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathcal{L}_{2,i}\mathcal{L}_{2,i}^{\top}\right\|\right]+2\sqrt{\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mathcal{L}_{1,i}\|^{2}\right]}\sqrt{\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mathcal{L}_{2,i}\|^{2}\right]}.

(B.112)

Given Lemmas
B.8
and
B.15
, the remaining is to establish the bound for
\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mathcal{L}_{1,i}\|^{2}\right]
. We first bound the moment for
\|\bm{\theta}_{k}\|
. Based on its definition (
B.20a
) and Lemma
3.5
, we have

\bm{\theta}_{k}=-(I-\widetilde{K}_{k})B_{k}^{-1}(g_{k}-\nabla f_{k})+(\widetilde{K}_{k}-K_{k})B_{k}^{-1}\nabla f_{k}.

Furthermore, by Assumption
3.2
with
q_{g}=2
, Lemma
3.7
, (
A.24
), (
A.5
) and the condition on
\tau
, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{\theta}_{k}\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\gamma_{H}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\|g_{k}-\nabla f_{k}\|^{2}\right]+\frac{1}{\gamma_{H}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\nabla f_{k}\|^{2}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{x}_{k}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{2}\right]+1+\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{x}_{k}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim 1\quad(\text{ by Lemma~\ref{sec4:lem1}}).

(B.113)

Since
(\bm{\theta}_{k})
is a martingale difference sequence, we follow (
B.14
) and get

\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mathcal{L}_{1,i}\|^{2}\right]\leq\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i}}\sum_{k=0}^{i}\varphi_{k}^{2}\prod_{l=k+1}^{i}(1-\varphi_{l}\lambda_{m})^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{\theta}_{k}\|^{2}\right]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{appen:A5:equ2}}}{{\lesssim}}\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i}}\cdot\varphi_{i}\lesssim 1,

(B.114)

where the second inequality also uses
41
. Combining the above display, Lemmas
B.8
and
B.15
, and plugging them into (
B.112
), we obtain

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i-1}}(\bm{x}_{i}-\bm{x}^{\star})(\bm{x}_{i}-\bm{x}^{\star})^{\top}-\Sigma^{\star}\right\|\right]\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{t\varphi_{t}}}+\sqrt{\varphi_{t}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{t\varphi_{t}}}.

(B.115)

\bullet

Proof of (
B.80
).
By the decomposition (
B.78
), we have

\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i-1}}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{x}_{i}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{2}\right]\lesssim\sum_{k=1}^{2}\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mathcal{L}_{k,i}\|^{2}\right]\lesssim 1,

(B.116)

where the last inequality follows from (
B.114
) and Lemma
B.15
.

This completes the proof.

B.17
Proof of Lemma
B.15

For the term
\mathcal{L}_{2,i}
defined in (
B.77
), by the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, we have

\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mathcal{L}_{2,i}\|^{2}\right]\lesssim\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i}}\prod_{k=0}^{i}(1-\varphi_{k}\lambda_{m})^{2}\|\bm{x}_{0}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i}}\left(\sum_{k=0}^{i}\prod_{l=k+1}^{i}(1-\varphi_{l}\lambda_{m})\varphi_{k}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{\delta}_{k}\|^{2}\right]}\right)^{2}.

(B.117)

Then, we focus on the rate of
\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{\delta}_{k}\|^{2}\right]
. By the definition of
\bm{\delta}_{k}
in (
B.20b
), we have

\displaystyle\|\bm{\delta}_{k}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|K_{k}-K^{\star}\|^{2}\|\bm{x}_{k}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{2}+\|(B^{\star})^{-1}\|^{2}\|\nabla f_{k}-B^{\star}(\bm{x}_{k}-\bm{x}^{\star})\|^{2}+\|B_{k}^{-1}\|^{2}\|(B^{\star})^{-1}\|^{2}\|B_{k}-B^{\star}\|^{2}\|\nabla f_{k}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|B_{k}-B^{\star}\|^{2}\|\bm{x}_{k}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{2}+\|\bm{x}_{k}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{4}+\|B_{k}-B^{\star}\|^{2}\|\bm{x}_{k}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{2},

where the second inequality is due to Assumption
3.1
, (
A.24
), (
A.27
) and (
B.14
). Taking the expectation on both sides yields

\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{\delta}_{k}\|^{2}\right]\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\|B_{k}-B^{\star}\|^{2}\|\bm{x}_{k}-\bm{x}_{k}^{\star}\|^{2}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{x}_{k}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{4}\right]\\
\lesssim\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\|B_{k}-B^{\star}\|^{4}\right]}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{x}_{k}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{4}\right]}+\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{x}_{k}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{4}\right]\lesssim\varphi_{k}^{2},

(B.118)

where the last inequality follows from Lemma
4.5
. Plugging (
B.118
) into (
B.117
) gives us

\displaystyle\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mathcal{L}_{2,i}\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i}}\prod_{k=0}^{i}(1-\varphi_{k}\lambda_{m})^{2}\|\bm{x}_{0}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i}}\left(\sum_{k=0}^{i}\prod_{l=k+1}^{i}(1-\varphi_{l}\lambda_{m})\varphi_{k}^{2}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i}}o(\varphi_{i}^{2})+\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i}}\varphi_{i}^{2}\lesssim\varphi_{t},

(B.119)

where the last inequality is the direct application of
41
. We complete the proof.

B.18
Proof of Lemma
B.10

The first inequality is trivial. We only show the second inequality based on the decomposition (
B.6
). In particular, we decompose
\bar{\bm{x}}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star}
as

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{x}}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\sum_{k=0}^{i}\prod_{l=k+1}^{i}(I-\varphi_{l}\mathcal{R})\varphi_{k}\bm{\theta}_{k}

\displaystyle\quad+\left[\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\prod_{k=0}^{i}(I-\varphi_{k}\mathcal{R})(\bm{x}_{0}-\bm{x}^{\star})+\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\sum_{k=0}^{i}\prod_{l=k+1}^{i}(I-\varphi_{l}\mathcal{R})\varphi_{k}\bm{\delta}_{k}\right]\eqqcolon\mathcal{Z}_{1,t}+\mathcal{Z}_{2,t}.

(B.120)

For the term
\mathcal{Z}_{1,t}
, exchanging the indices gives us

\mathcal{Z}_{1,t}=\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\sum_{k=0}^{i}\prod_{l=k+1}^{i}(I-\varphi_{l}\mathcal{R})\varphi_{k}\bm{\theta}_{k}=\frac{1}{t}\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}\sum_{i=k}^{t-1}\prod_{l=k+1}^{i}(I-\varphi_{l}\mathcal{R})\varphi_{k}\bm{\theta}_{k}.

Since
(\bm{\theta}_{k})
is a martingale difference sequence, the interaction terms in
\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mathcal{Z}_{1,t}\|^{2}\right]
vanish. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mathcal{Z}_{1,t}\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{t^{2}}\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}\varphi_{k}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sum_{i=k}^{t-1}\prod_{l=k+1}^{i}(I-\varphi_{l}\mathcal{R})\bm{\theta}_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]\leq\frac{1}{t^{2}}\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}\left[\sum_{i=k}^{t-1}\prod_{l=k+1}^{i}(1-\varphi_{l}\lambda_{m})\right]^{2}\varphi_{k}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{\theta}_{k}\|^{2}\right]\eqqcolon(\#),

which can be rewritten by exchanging the indices as

\displaystyle(\#)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{t^{2}}\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}\sum_{i_{1}=k}^{t-1}\sum_{i_{2}=k}^{t-1}\prod_{l_{1}=k+1}^{i_{1}}(1-\varphi_{l_{1}}\lambda_{m})\prod_{l_{2}=k+1}^{i_{2}}(1-\varphi_{l_{2}}\lambda_{m})\varphi_{k}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{\theta}_{k}\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{t^{2}}\sum_{i_{1}=0}^{t-1}\sum_{i_{2}=0}^{t-1}\sum_{k=0}^{i_{1}\wedge i_{2}}\prod_{l_{1}=k+1}^{i_{1}}(1-\varphi_{l_{1}}\lambda_{m})\prod_{l_{2}=k+1}^{i_{2}}(1-\varphi_{l_{2}}\lambda_{m})\varphi_{k}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{\theta}_{k}\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{t^{2}}\sum_{i_{1}=0}^{t-1}\sum_{i_{2}=0}^{i_{1}}\prod_{l_{1}=i_{2}+1}^{i_{1}}(1-\varphi_{l_{1}}\lambda_{m})\sum_{k=0}^{i_{2}}\prod_{l_{2}=k+1}^{i_{2}}(1-\varphi_{l_{2}}\lambda_{m})^{2}\varphi_{k}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{\theta}_{k}\|^{2}\right],

where the last inequality is due to the symmetry between the indices
i_{1}
and
i_{2}
. We plug in (
B.16
) and get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mathcal{Z}_{1,t}\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{t^{2}}\sum_{i_{1}=0}^{t-1}\sum_{i_{2}=0}^{i_{1}}\prod_{l_{1}=i_{2}+1}^{i_{1}}(1-\varphi_{l_{1}}\lambda_{m})\sum_{k=0}^{i_{2}}\prod_{l_{2}=k+1}^{i_{2}}(1-\varphi_{l_{2}}\lambda_{m})^{2}\varphi_{k}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{t^{2}}\sum_{i_{1}=0}^{t-1}\sum_{i_{2}=0}^{i_{1}}\prod_{l_{1}=i_{2}+1}^{i_{1}}(1-\varphi_{l_{1}}\lambda_{m})\varphi_{i_{2}}\lesssim\frac{1}{t^{2}}\sum_{i_{1}=0}^{t-1}1=\frac{1}{t},

(B.121)

where the last two inequalities are due to
41
. For the term
\mathcal{Z}_{2,t}
, similarly to the analysis in (
B.117
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mathcal{Z}_{2,t}\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\prod_{k=0}^{i}(1-\varphi_{k}\lambda_{m})\right)^{2}\|\bm{x}_{0}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{2}+\left(\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\sum_{k=0}^{i}\prod_{l=k+1}^{i}(1-\varphi_{l}\lambda_{m})\varphi_{k}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{\delta}_{k}\|^{2}\right]}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{appen:A5:equ6}}}}{{\lesssim}}\;\left(\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\prod_{k=0}^{i}(1-\varphi_{k}\lambda_{m})\right)^{2}\|\bm{x}_{0}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{2}+\left(\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\sum_{k=0}^{i}\prod_{l=k+1}^{i}(1-\varphi_{l}\lambda_{m})\varphi_{k}^{2}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}o(\varphi_{i})\right)+\left(\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\varphi_{i}\right)^{2}\lesssim\varphi_{t}^{2},

(B.122)

where the third inequality is again due to
41
. Combining (
B.18
), (
B.18
) and (
B.18
) together yields

\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i}}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bar{\bm{x}}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\star}\|^{2}\right]\lesssim\frac{1}{t^{2}}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\frac{1}{\varphi_{i}}\lesssim\frac{1}{t\varphi_{t}},

(B.123)

where the last inequality is due to the fact that
\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}1/\varphi_{i}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}i^{\varphi}di\lesssim t/\varphi_{t}
. This completes the proof.

Appendix C
Additional Experimental Results

In this section, we follow the experiments in Section
5
and introduce detailed experimental setups along with additional experimental results.

C.1
Detailed experimental setup

For the linear regression problem, we consider the model
\xi_{b}=\xi_{a}^{\top}\bm{x}^{\star}+\varepsilon
, where
\varepsilon\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
is Gaussian noise. For this model, we use the squared loss
F(\bm{x};\xi)=0.5(\xi_{b}-\xi_{a}^{\top}\bm{x})^{2}
.

For the logistic regression problem, we consider the model
P(\xi_{b}|\xi_{a})=\frac{\text{exp}(\xi_{b}\cdot\xi_{a}^{\top}\bm{x}^{\star})}{1+\text{exp}(\xi_{b}\cdot\xi_{a}^{\top}\bm{x}^{\star})}
with
\xi_{b}\in\{-1,1\}
.
For this model, we use the log loss
F(\bm{x};\xi)=\log(1+\exp(-\xi_{b}\cdot\xi_{a}^{\top}\bm{x}))
.

\bullet

Model parameters setup.

For both regression tasks, we consider dimensions
d\in\{20,40\}
and set the ground-truth model parameter
\bm{x}^{\star}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
to be linearly spaced between
0
and
1
. For the linear model, we fix the noise variance at
\sigma^{2}=1
. For each dimension
d
, we generate the covariate
\xi_{a}\sim\mathcal{N}({\bm{0}},\Sigma_{a})
with three different types of
\Sigma_{a}
. (i) Identity matrix:
\Sigma_{a}=I
; (ii) Equi-correlation matrix:
[\Sigma_{a}]_{i,i}=1
and
[\Sigma_{a}]_{i,j}=r
for
i\neq j
; and (iii) Toeplitz matrix:
[\Sigma_{a}]_{i,j}=r^{|i-j|}
. For the latter two covariance matrices, we set
r=0.4
.

\bullet

Algorithm parameters setup.

We vary the number of sketching steps
\tau\in\{5,10,\infty\}
, where
\tau=\infty
corresponds to solving (
2.2
) exactly. For the sketching solver, we apply both randomized Kaczmarz sampling
S\sim\text{Uniform}(\{\bm{e}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{d})

(
54
)
and Gaussian sampling
S\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})
. We also set
(\alpha_{t},\beta_{t},\gamma_{t})
according to the formulas in (
2.4
). Since
\mu_{t}
and
\nu_{t}
are generally intractable in practice, we consider two implementable choices. First (the default choice), at each iteration we approximate the expectations in the definitions of
\mu_{t}
and
\nu_{t}
using empirical averages based on
B_{t}
(see (
2.5
)). Second (the degenerate choice), we adopt the simplified specification
\mu_{t}\nu_{t}=\gamma_{t}=1
for all
t
. For the online Newton method, we use a diminishing stepsize
\varphi_{t}=1/(t+1)^{0.501}
. We set the nominal confidence level to
95\%
, and perform inference on the coordinate-wise mean of
\bm{x}^{\star}
, i.e.
\sum_{i=1}^{d}\bm{x}_{i}^{\star}/d
. For each parameter choice, we conduct 200 independent runs.

C.2
Additional results and discussions

In this section, we present and discuss additional experimental results. Table
2
reports numerical experiments for
d=20
on both linear and logistic regression problems. In terms of asymptotic validity, the empirical coverage rates consistently remain close to the nominal level of
95\%
across all parameter configurations. The only exception is the linear regression case under Gaussian sketching. When
\Sigma_{a}
is the identity and the number of sketching steps
\tau=5
, the empirical coverage rate drops to
90.5\%
, which is slightly below the nominal level.
This mild undercoverage may be attributed to the estimation error in the weighted sample covariance estimator
\widehat{\Sigma}_{t}
and the approximation error introduced by the sketching solver when
\tau
is small. These numerical effects may lead to narrower confidence intervals in finite samples. Nevertheless, the observed coverage rates remain within an acceptable range for practical inference. Overall, the close alignment with the target coverage level provides numerical validations for the asymptotic normality established in Theorem
4.3
and confirms the consistency of the proposed weighted sample covariance estimator in Theorem
4.6
. Notably, even with a limited number of sketching steps (
\tau=5
), the coverage rates remain largely comparable to those achieved by the exact Newton method (
\tau=\infty
) in almost all cases, demonstrating the robustness of the accelerated sketching approach for statistical inference.

In terms of the mean absolute error, our proposed Newton method achieves comparable iteration error with the exact Newton method even with a small sketching steps
\tau
. Similar to the case with
d=40
, in some particular settings, e.g.,
\Sigma_{a}
is Toeplitz or Equi-correlation in linear regression problems, the iteration error of the inexact Newton method is even smaller than that of the exact Newton method. Furthermore, the average confidence interval lengths exhibit only modest increases when the sketching solver is applied. Thus, these results indicate that our proposed inexact Newton method preserves statistical efficiency.

.

Finally, we present additional experiments on QQ plots. Figures
2
and
3
report quantile–quantile comparisons between the Newton method with accelerated sketching and the Newton method with unaccelerated sketching for logistic regression, under two sketching distributions: randomized Kaczmarz sketching (Figure
2
) and Gaussian sketching (Figure
3
).
Across all configurations, the scatter points closely align with the 45-degree reference line, indicating that the quantiles of the accelerated sketching scheme closely match those of the unaccelerated sketching scheme. This close agreement is observed consistently for both sketching distributions (Kaczmarz and Gaussian), across different problem dimensions (
d=10,20
), and for varying numbers of sketching steps (
\tau=3,5
). The alignment persists even in the degenerate case
\gamma=\mu\nu=1
, which is consistent with Proposition
4.4
(b). These results lead to two conclusions for logistic regression problems. First, the proposed Newton method with accelerated sketching satisfies the asymptotic normality established in Theorem
4.3
. Second, Nesterov’s acceleration does not degrade statistical efficiency, as the limiting covariance of the accelerated sketching scheme remains comparable to that of the unaccelerated sketching scheme.
